<p>2B.56 WALK ON LEFT FACING TRAFFIC and No Hitchhiking Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-1.svg">R9-1</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-4.svg">R9-4</a>, and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-4a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-4a.svg">R9-4a</a>) 2B.57 Pedestrian Crossing Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-2.svg">R9-2</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r9-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R9-3.svg">R9-3</a>) 2B.58 Traffic Signal Pedestrian and Bicycle Actuation Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-1.svg">R10-1</a> through <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-4.svg">R10-4</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-24/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-24.svg">R10-24</a> through R1-26)</p>

<div class="subch">Traffic Signal Signs</div>

<p>2B.59 Traffic Signal Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-5.svg">R10-5</a> through <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-30/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-30.svg">R10-30</a>) 2B.60 No Turn on Red Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-11/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-11.svg">R10-11</a> Series, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-17a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-17a.svg">R10-17a</a>, and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-30/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-30.svg">R10-30</a>) 2B.61 Ramp Metering Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-28/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-28.svg">R10-28</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-29/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-29.svg">R10-29</a>)</p>

<div class="subch">Road Closed And Weight Limit Signs</div>

<p>2B.62 KEEP OFF MEDIAN Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-1.svg">R11-1</a>) 2B.63 ROAD CLOSED Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-2.svg">R11-2</a>) and LOCAL TRAFFIC ONLY Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-3.svg">R11-3</a> Series and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r11-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R11-4.svg">R11-4</a>) 2B.64 Weight Limit Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-1.svg">R12-1</a> through <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-7.svg">R12-7</a>) 2B.65 Weigh Station Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r13-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R13-1.svg">R13-1</a>) 2B.66 TRUCK ROUTE Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-1.svg">R14-1</a>) 2B.67 Hazardous Material Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-2.svg">R14-2</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-3.svg">R14-3</a>) 2B.68 National Network Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-4.svg">R14-4</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-5.svg">R14-5</a>)</p>

<div class="subch">Other Regulatory Signs And Plaques</div>

<p>2B.69 Photo Enforced Signs and Plaques (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18.svg">R10-18</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18a.svg">R10-18a</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-19p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-19P.svg">R10-19P</a>, and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-19ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-19aP.svg">R10-19aP</a>) 2B.70 MINOR CRASHES MOVE VEHICLES FROM TRAVEL LANES Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-4.svg">R16-4</a>) 2B.71 Move Over or Reduced Speed Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-3.svg">R16-3</a>) 2B.72 No Hand-Held Phone Use by Driver Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-15/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-15.svg">R16-15</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-15a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-15a.svg">R16-15a</a>) 2B.73 Headlight Use Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-5.svg">R16-5</a> through <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-11/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-11.svg">R16-11</a>) 2B.74 Seat Belt Symbol</p>

<div class="subch">Barricades And Gates</div>

<p>2B.75 Barricades 2B.76 Gates</p>

<div class="subch">California Sections</div>

<p><span class="ca">2B.101(CA)  NO FISHING (JUMPING) FROM BRIDGE Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r23ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r23ca.webp">R23(CA)</a>)  2B.102(CA)  $1000 Fine Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r47ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r47ca.webp">R47(CA)</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r47aca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r47aca.webp">R47A(CA)</a>)  2B.103(CA)  PRIVATE ROAD (PRIVATE PROPERTY) VEHICLE CODE ENFORCED Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r101ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r101ca.webp">R101(CA)</a>)  2B.104(CA)  GOLF CARTS OK DAYLIGHT HOURS Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr43ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr43ca.webp">SR43(CA)</a>)  2B.105(CA)  Bus and Truck Registration Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr44ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr44ca.webp">SR44(CA)</a>)  2B.106(CA)  EMERGENCY ACCESS KEEP CLEAR Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr46ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr46ca.webp">SR46(CA)</a>)  2B.107(CA)  Off Highway Vehicle Signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr47ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr47ca.webp">SR47(CA)</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr48ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr48ca.webp">SR48(CA)</a>)  2B.108(CA)  NO CAMPING OR SLEEPING IN VEHICLE Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r119ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r119ca.webp">R119(CA)</a>)  2B.109(CA)  BEGIN/END FREEWAY Signs (R57(CA) and R58(CA))  2B.110(CA)  No Loitering, Camping, Vending or Parking of Vehicles 30 Feet or Longer Sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/s22ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/s22ca.webp">S22(CA)</a>) </span></p>

<div class="subch">General</div>


<span id="2b01" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.01 Application of Regulatory Signs

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.01 &middot; manual page 156 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.01/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Regulatory signs shall be used to inform road users of selected traffic laws or regulations and to indicate the applicability of the legal requirements.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Regulatory signs shall be installed at or near where the regulations apply. The signs shall clearly indicate the requirements imposed by the regulations and shall be designed and installed to provide adequate visibility and legibility in order to obtain compliance.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Regulatory signs shall be retroreflective or illuminated (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a21">Section 2A.21</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Traffic control device related orders, ordinances and resolutions by local authorities which affect State highways shall  be approved by Caltrans.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Signs required for enforcement are normally placed by, and at the expense of, the authority establishing the regulation.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Refer to CVC § 21461 for failure to obey a regulatory sign.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b02" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.02 Design of Regulatory Signs

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.02 &middot; manual page 156 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.02/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Regulatory signs shall be rectangular unless specifically designated otherwise in this Manual. Regulatory signs shall be designed in accordance with the sizes, shapes, colors, and legends contained in the “Standard Highway Signs” publication <span class="ca">and Caltrans’ California Sign Specifications</span> (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/1a/#1a05">Section 1A.05</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The use of educational plaques to supplement symbol signs is described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a09">Section 2A.09</a>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The use of LEDs in the border or legend of regulatory signs is described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a12">Section 2A.12</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> LED signs displaying a part-time prohibitory message incorporating a red circle and diagonal of a static sign shall display a red symbol that approximates the same red circle and diagonal as closely as possible. The symbol of the action to be prohibited shall be displayed in white LEDs on a black background.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> A regulatory sign displayed entirely with LEDs and incorporated within the border of a larger full-matrix changeable message sign shall display the regulatory sign legend in the size, shape, color, and legend of the standard regulatory sign.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b03" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.03 Size of Regulatory Signs

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.03 &middot; manual page 156 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.03/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Except as provided in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a07">Section 2A.07</a>, the minimum sizes for regulatory signs shall be as shown in <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a> <span class="ca">and <a href="#fig-2b-1ca">Table 2B-1(CA)</a></span>.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-inline single"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-1"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0288.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0288.webp" alt="Table 2B-1. Regulatory Sign and Plaque Sizes"></a><figcaption>Table 2B-1. Regulatory Sign and Plaque Sizes <span class="src-page">p 288</span></figcaption></figure></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a07">Section 2A.07</a> contains information regarding the applicability of the various columns in <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Except as provided in Paragraphs 5 and 6 of this Section, the minimum sizes for regulatory signs facing traffic on multi-lane conventional roads shall be as shown in the Multi-Lane column of <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a><span class="ca"> and <a href="#fig-2b-1ca">Table 2B-1(CA)</a></span>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The minimum size of regulatory signs applied on low-volume rural roads with operating speeds of 30 mph or less shall be as shown in the Minimum column of <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Where the posted speed limit is 35 mph or less on a multi-lane highway or street, other than for a STOP sign, the minimum size shown in the Single Lane column in <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a><span class="ca">and 2B-1(CA)</span> may be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Where a regulatory sign, other than a STOP sign, is placed on the left-hand side of a multi-lane roadway in addition to the installation of the same regulatory sign on the right-hand side or the roadway, the minimum size shown in the Single Lane column in <a href="#fig-2b-1">Table 2B-1</a><span class="ca"> and 2B-1(CA)</span> may be used for both the sign on the right- hand side and the sign on the left-hand side of the roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The minimum sizes for regulatory signs facing traffic on exit and entrance ramps at major interchanges connecting an Expressway or Freeway with an Expressway or Freeway (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2e/#2e11">Section 2E.11</a>) should be as shown in the column of</p>
</div>

<span id="2b05" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.05 YIELD Sign (R1-2)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.05 &middot; manual page 158 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.05/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The YIELD sign requires road users to yield the right-of-way to other traffic on certain approaches to an intersection or on a two way approach to a one way section of roadway, such as a narrow bridge or underpass. Vehicles controlled by a YIELD sign need to slow down to a speed that is reasonable for the existing conditions or stop when necessary to avoid interfering with conflicting traffic.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The YIELD (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2.svg">R1-2</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-1">Figure 2B-1</a>) shall not be displayed using a changeable message sign.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Signing For Right-Of-Way At Intersections</div>


<span id="2b06" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.06 General Considerations

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.06 &middot; manual page 159 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.06/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Unsignalized intersections represent the most common form of intersection right-of-way control. Selection of control type might be impacted by specific requirements of State law or local ordinances.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Roundabouts and traffic circles are circular intersection designs and are not traffic control devices. The decision to convert an intersection from a conventional intersection to a circular intersection is an engineering design decision and not a traffic control device decision. As such, criteria for conversion from a conventional intersection to a circular intersection are not included in the MUTCD.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The type of traffic control used at an unsignalized intersection should be the least restrictive that provides appropriate levels of safety and efficiency for all road users.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Some types of right-of-way control that can exist at an unsignalized intersection in order from the least restrictive to the most restrictive are the following: A. No intersection control (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b09">Section 2B.09</a>): There are no right-of-way traffic control devices on any of the approaches to the intersection. B. Yield control (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b10">Section 2B.10</a>): YIELD signs are placed on all approaches (for a circular intersection), on opposing approaches for a four-leg intersection, on a single approach for a three-leg intersection, or in the median of a divided highway. The YIELD signs are placed on the minor road. C. Minor road stop control (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b11">Section 2B.11</a>): STOP signs are typically placed on opposing approaches (for a four-leg intersection) or on a single approach (for a three-leg intersection). The STOP signs are normally placed on the minor road. <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b07">Section 2B.07</a> contains guidance on selecting the minor road. D. All-way stop control (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b12">Section 2B.12</a>): STOP signs are placed on all approaches to the intersection.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> When selecting a form of intersection control, the following factors should be considered: A. Motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian traffic volumes on all approaches; where the term units/day or units/hour is indicated, it should be the total of motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian volume; B. Driver yielding behavior with regard to all modes of conflicting traffic, including bicyclists and pedestrians; C. Number and angle of approaches; E. Approach speeds; E. Sight distance available on each approach; F. Reported crash experience; and G. The presence of a grade crossing near the intersection.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> YIELD or STOP signs shall not be used for speed control.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Appropriate traffic calming or other speed control measures are available to control vehicle speeds, such as those that do not have the potential to diminish the effectiveness of traffic control devices when used for their specified purpose.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> Because the potential for conflicting commands could create driver confusion, YIELD or STOP signs shall not be used in conjunction with any traffic control signal operation, except in the following cases: A. If the signal indication for an approach is a flashing red at all times; B. If a minor street or driveway is located within or adjacent to the area controlled by the traffic control signal, but does not require separate traffic signal control because an extremely low potential for conflict exists; or C. If a channelized turn lane is separated from the adjacent travel lanes by an island and the channelized turn lane is not controlled by a traffic control signal.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08a</span> <span class="ca">STOP signs shall not be erected at any entrance to an intersection controlled by traffic signals. Refer to  CVC § 21355(a).</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">08b</span> <span class="ca">STOP signs may be used at a channelized turn lane if it is separated from the adjacent travel lanes moving in same direction by  an island and the channelized turn lane is not controlled by a traffic control signal.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> STOP signs and YIELD signs shall not be installed on different approaches to the same unsignalized intersection if those approaches conflict with or oppose each other, except as provided for in Items A and B in Paragraph 3 of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b10">Section 2B.10</a>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> Portable or part-time STOP or YIELD signs shall not be used except for emergency and temporary traffic control zone purposes.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> A portable or part-time (folding) STOP sign that is manually placed into view and manually removed from view shall not be used during a power outage to control a signalized approach unless the maintaining agency establishes that the signal indication that will first be displayed to that approach upon restoration of power is a flashing red signal indication and that the portable STOP sign will be manually removed from view prior to resuming stop-and-go operation of the traffic control signal.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> A portable or part-time (folding) STOP sign that is electrically or mechanically operated such that it only displays the stop message during a power outage and ceases to display the stop message upon restoration of power may be used during a power outage to control a signalized approach.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> The use of STOP signs at grade crossings is described in Sections 8B.04 and 8B.05.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/9b/#9b01">Section 9B.01</a> contains provisions regarding the assignment of priority where a shared-use path crosses a roadway.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b07" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.07 Determining the Minor Road for Unsignalized Intersections

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.07 &middot; manual page 160 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.07/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The selection of the minor road to be controlled by YIELD or STOP signs should be based on one or more of the following criteria: A.A roadway intersecting a designated through or numbered highway, B.A roadway with the lower functional classification, C.A roadway with the lower traffic volume, D.A roadway with the lower speed limit, and/or E.A roadway that intersects with a roadway that has a higher priority for one or more modes of travel.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> When two roadways that have relatively equal volumes, speeds, and/or other characteristics intersect, the following factors should be considered in selecting the minor road for installation of YIELD or STOP signs: A.Controlling the direction that conflicts the most with established pedestrian crossing activity or school walking routes; B.Controlling the direction that has obscured vision, dips, or bumps that already require drivers to use lower operating speeds; and C.Controlling the direction that has the best sight distance from a controlled position to observe conflicting traffic.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Refer to CVC § 21356 for the erection of YIELD signs upon the approaches to more than one of the intersecting streets.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b08" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.08 Right-of-Way Intersection Control Considerations

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.08 &middot; manual page 160 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.08/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Before converting to a more restrictive form of right-of-way control at an unsignalized intersection, the following alternative treatments to address safety, operational, or other concerns should be among those to be considered: A.Where yield or stop controlled, installing Yield Ahead or Stop Ahead signs on the appropriate approaches to the intersection; B.Removing parking on one or more approaches; C.Removing sight distance obstructions; D.Installing signs along the major street to warn road users approaching the intersection; E.Relocating the stop line(s) and making other changes to improve the sight distance at the intersection; F.Installing measures designed to reduce speeds on the approaches; G.Installing an Intersection Control Beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/4s/#4s02">Section 4S.02</a>) or Stop Beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/4s/#4s05">Section 4S.05</a>) at the intersection to supplement STOP sign control; H.Installing a Warning Beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/4s/#4s03">Section 4S.03</a>) on warning signs in advance of a stop-controlled intersection on major-street and/or minor-street approaches; I.Adding one or more lanes on a minor-street approach to reduce the number of vehicles per lane on the approach; J.Revising the geometrics at the intersection to channelize vehicular movements and reduce the time required for a vehicle to complete a movement, which could also assist pedestrians; K.Revising the geometrics at the intersection to add pedestrian median refuge islands and/or curb extensions; L.Installing roadway lighting if a disproportionate number of crashes occur at night; M.Restricting one or more turning movements on a full-time or part-time basis if alternate routes are available; N.Installing on the major street a pedestrian-actuated device: Warning Beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/4s/#4s03">Section 4S.03</a>), rectangular rapid-flashing beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/4l/#4l01">Section 4L.01</a>), or In-Roadway Warning Lights (see Chapter 4U), if pedestrian safety is the major concern; O.If the warrant is satisfied, installing all-way stop control; P.Installing a pedestrian hybrid beacon (see Chapter 4J) on the major street to address pedestrian safety; Q.Installing a circular intersection; and R.Employing other alternatives, depending on conditions at the intersection.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b09" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.09 No Intersection Control

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.09 &middot; manual page 161 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.09/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The decision not to use intersection control should be based on engineering judgment.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The following factors may be considered: A.Intersection sight distance is adequate on all approaches. B.All approaches to the intersection are a single lane and there are no separate turn lanes. C.The combined motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian volume (existing or projected) entering the intersection from all approaches averages less than 1,000 units per day or 80 units in the peak hour. D.There are no marked crosswalks or bicycle lanes on any approach. E.None of the approaches to the intersection are for a through highway, main road, or higher functional classification. F.The angle of intersection is between 90 and 75 degrees. G.The functional classification of the intersecting streets is either the intersection of two local streets or the intersection of a local street with a collector street.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b10" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.10 Yield Control

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.10 &middot; manual page 161 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.10/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> At intersections where a full stop is not necessary at all times, consideration should first be given to using less restrictive measures such as YIELD signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Yield control should be considered when engineering judgment indicates that all of the following conditions exist: A.Intersection sight distance is adequate on the approaches to be controlled by YIELD signs. B.All approaches to the intersection are a single lane and there are no separate turn lanes. C.One of the following crash-related criteria applies: D. For changing from no intersection control to yield control, there have been two or more reported crashes in the previous 12 months that are susceptible to correction by the installation of a YIELD sign. E. For changing from minor road stop control to yield control, there have been two or fewer reported crashes in the previous 12 months. F. The combined motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian volume entering the intersection averages less than 1,800 units per day or 140 units in the peak hour. G. The angle of intersection is between 90 and 75 degrees. H. The functional classification of the intersecting streets is either the intersection of two local streets or the intersection of a local street with a collector street.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02a</span> <span class="ca">Refer to FHWA’s List of Known Errors for errors in Paragraph 2, Items D and E text. Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/1a/#1a04">Section 1A.04</a> for more details.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> YIELD signs may be installed at an intersection when any of the following conditions apply: A.At the second intersection of a divided highway crossing or median break functioning as two separate intersections (see <a href="#fig-2b-19">Figure 2B-19</a>). In this case, a YIELD sign may be installed at the entrance to the second intersection. B.For a channelized turn lane that is separated from the adjacent travel lanes by an island, even if the adjacent lanes at the intersection are controlled by a highway traffic control signal or by a STOP sign. C.At an intersection where a special problem exists and where engineering judgment indicates the problem to be susceptible to correction by the use of the YIELD sign. D.Facing the entering roadway for a merge-type movement if engineering judgment indicates that control is needed because acceleration geometry and/or sight distance is not adequate for merging traffic operation. E.On low-volume rural roads if engineering judgment indicates that a YIELD sign would provide adequate control. F.On an approach to an intersection where the only permissible movement is a right-turn movement with an intersection geometry similar to a channelized right-turn lane or an approach to a roundabout.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-inline single"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-19"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0260.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0260.webp" alt="Figure 2B-19. ONE WAY Signing for Divided Highway Crossings that Function as Two Separate Intersections"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-19. ONE WAY Signing for Divided Highway Crossings that Function as Two Separate Intersections <span class="src-page">p 260</span></figcaption></figure></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The YIELD signs should be installed on opposing minor-street approaches (for a four-leg intersection) or on the minor-street approach (for a three-leg intersection). When two intersecting roadways have relatively equal volumes, speeds, and other characteristics, yield control should be installed on the approach that conflicts the most with established pedestrian crossing activity, school walking routes, or bicycle crossing activity.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> A YIELD sign shall be used to require road users to yield the right-of-way to other traffic at the entrance to a roundabout. YIELD signs at roundabouts shall be used to control the approach roadways and shall not be used to control the circulatory roadway.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> YIELD signs shall not be placed <span class="ca">in more than one of</span> on all of the approaches to an intersection, except at roundabouts. <span class="ca">Refer to CVC § 21356.</span></p>
</div>

<span id="2b11" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.11 Minor Road Stop Control

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.11 &middot; manual page 162 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.11/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Stop control on the minor-road approach or approaches to an intersection should be considered when engineering judgment indicates that one or more of the following conditions exist: A.A restricted view exists that requires road users to stop in order to adequately observe conflicting traffic on the through street or highway. B.Crash records indicate that: 1.For a four-leg intersection, there are three or more reported crashes in a 12-month period or six or more reported crashes in a 36-month period. The crashes should be susceptible to correction by installation of minor-road stop control. 2.For a three-leg intersection, there are three or more reported crashes in a 12-month period or five or more reported crashes in a 36-month period. The crashes should be susceptible to correction by installation of minor-road stop control. C.The intersection is of a lower functional classification road with a higher functional classification road. D.Conditions that previously supported the installation of all-way stop control no longer exist.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> On low-volume rural roads, a STOP sign should be considered at an intersection where engineering judgment indicates that Item C in Paragraph 1 of this Section is applicable or where the intersection has inadequate sight distance for the operating vehicle speeds.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b12" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.12 All-Way Stop Control

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.12 &middot; manual page 163 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.12/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The provisions in the following sections describe warrants for the recommended engineering study to determine all-way stop control. Warrants are not a substitute for engineering judgment. The fact that a warrant for a particular traffic control device is met is not conclusive justification to install or not install all-way stop control. Because each intersection will have unique characteristics that affect its operational performance or safety, it is the engineering study for a given intersection that is ultimately the basis for a decision to install or not install all-way stop control.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> All-way stop controls at intersections with substantially differing approach volumes can reduce the effectiveness of these devices for all roadway users.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The decision to establish all-way stop control at an unsignalized intersection should be based on an engineering study. The engineering study for all-way stop control should include an analysis of factors related to the existing operation and safety at the intersection, the potential to improve these conditions, and the applicable factors contained in the following all-way stop control warrants: A.All-Way Stop Control Warrant A: Crash Experience (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b13">Section 2B.13</a>) B.All-Way Stop Control Warrant B: Sight Distance (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b14">Section 2B.14</a>) C.All-Way Stop Control Warrant C: Transition to Signal Control or Transition to Yield Control at a Circular Intersection (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b15">Section 2B.15</a>) D.All-Way Stop Control Warrant D: 8-Hour Volume (Vehicles, Pedestrians, Bicycles) (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b16">Section 2B.16</a>) E.All-Way Stop Control Warrant E: Other Factors (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b17">Section 2B.17</a>)</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The decision to install all-way stop control on site roadways open to public travel may be based on engineering judgment.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The satisfaction of an all-way stop control warrant or warrants shall not in itself require the installation of all-way stop control at an unsignalized intersection.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b13" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.13 All-Way Stop Control Warrant A: Crash Experience

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.13 &middot; manual page 163 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.13/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> All-way stop control may be installed at an intersection where an engineering study indicates that: A.For a four-leg intersection, there are five or more reported crashes in a 12-month period or six or more reported crashes in a 36-month period that were of a type susceptible to correction by the installation of all-way stop control. B.For a three-leg intersection, there are four or more reported crashes in a 12-month period or five or more reported crashes in a 36-month period that were of a type susceptible to correction by the installation of all-way stop control.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b14" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.14 All-Way Stop Control Warrant B: Sight Distance

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.14 &middot; manual page 163 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.14/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> All-way stop control may be installed at an intersection where an engineering study indicates that sight distance on the minor-road approaches controlled by a STOP sign is not adequate for a vehicle to turn onto or cross the major (uncontrolled) road.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> At such a location, a road user, after stopping, cannot see conflicting traffic and is not able to negotiate the intersection unless conflicting cross traffic is also required to stop.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b15" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.15 All-Way Stop Control Warrant C: Transition to Signal Control or Transition to Yield Control at a Circular Intersection

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.15 &middot; manual page 163 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.15/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> All-way stop control may be installed at locations where all-way stop control is an interim measure that can be installed to control traffic while arrangements are being made for the installation of a traffic control signal (see Chapter 4C) at the intersection or for the installation of yield control at a circular intersection.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b16" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.16 All-Way Stop Control Warrant D: 8-Hour Volume (Vehicles, Pedestrians, Bicycles)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.16 &middot; manual page 164 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.16/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> All-way stop control may be installed at an intersection where an engineering study indicates: A.The combined motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian volume entering the intersection from the major- street approaches is at least 300 units per hour for each of any 8 hours of a typical day; and B.The combined motor vehicle, bicycle, and pedestrian volume entering the intersection from the minor- street approaches is at least 200 units per hour for each of any of the same 8 hours.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If the 85th-percentile approach speed of the major-street traffic exceeds 40 mph, the minimum vehicular volume warrants may be reduced to 70 percent of the values given in Items A and B in Paragraph 1 of this Section.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b17" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.17 All-Way Stop Control Warrant E: Other Factors

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.17 &middot; manual page 164 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.17/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> All-way stop control may be installed at an intersection where an engineering study indicates that all- way stop control is needed due to other factors not addressed in the other all-way stop control warrants. Such other factors may include, but are not limited to, the following: A.The need to control left-turn conflicts, B.An intersection of two residential neighborhood collector (through) streets of similar design and operating characteristics where all-way stop control would improve traffic operational characteristics of the intersection, or C.Where pedestrian and/or bicyclist movements support the installation of all-way stop control.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b18" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.18 STOP Sign or YIELD Sign Placement

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.18 &middot; manual page 164 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.18/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The STOP or YIELD sign shall be installed on the near side of the intersection on the right- hand side of the approach to which it applies. When the STOP or YIELD sign is installed at this required location and the sign visibility is restricted, a Stop Ahead sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c35">Section 2C.35</a>) shall be installed in advance of the STOP sign or a Yield Ahead sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c35">Section 2C.35</a>) shall be installed in advance of the YIELD sign.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The STOP or YIELD sign shall be located as close as practicable to the intersection it regulates, while optimizing its visibility to the road user it is intended to regulate.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> STOP signs and YIELD signs shall not be mounted on the same post.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03a</span> <span class="ca">If other signs are grouped with a STOP sign, except for ONE WAY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-1__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R6-1</a> &amp; <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-2.svg">R6-2</a>) signs and Street Name (D3-1) sign  (refer to Sections 2B.49 &amp; 2D.45), they shall be installed below the STOP sign.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">03b</span> <span class="ca">When a Street Name sign and a ONE WAY sign are mounted on the same assembly as a STOP sign, the Street Name sign  needs to be placed at the top, the ONE WAY sign directly below it, and the STOP sign at the bottom.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a05">Section 2A.05</a> contains information about mounting signs back-to-back with a STOP or YIELD sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> STOP or YIELD signs should not be placed farther than 50 feet from the edge of the pavement of the intersected roadway (see Drawing F in <a href="#fig-2a-3">Figure 2A-3</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Supplemental plaques used in conjunction with a STOP or YIELD sign should be limited to those specified for such use in this Manual</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Where drivers proceeding straight ahead must yield to traffic approaching from the opposite direction, such as at a one-lane bridge, a TO ONCOMING TRAFFIC (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2aP.svg">R1-2aP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-1">Figure 2B-1</a>) may be mounted below the YIELD sign.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> Where drivers must yield to traffic in a multi-lane roundabout, a TO TRAFFIC IN CIRCLE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2bP.svg">R1-2bP</a>) or TO ALL LANES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2cp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2cP.svg">R1-2cP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-1">Figure 2B-1</a>) may be mounted below the YIELD sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> <a href="#fig-2a-3">Figure 2A-3</a> shows examples of some typical placements of STOP signs and YIELD signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">09a</span> <span class="ca">The TO ONCOMING TRAFFIC (R1-2a) sign when used, shall be mounted on the same post and immediately below a  YIELD (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2.svg">R1-2</a>) sign.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">09b</span> <span class="ca">The width of the R1-2a sign should be equal to the width of the YIELD (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2.svg">R1-2</a>) sign.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a13">Section 2A.13</a> contains additional information about separate and combined mounting of other signs with STOP or YIELD signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> Stop lines that are used to supplement a STOP sign should be located as described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/3b/#3b19">Section 3B.19</a>. Yield lines that are used to supplement a YIELD sign should be located as described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/3b/#3b19">Section 3B.19</a>.</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> Where there is a marked crosswalk at the intersection, the STOP sign should be installed in advance of the edge of the crosswalk that is nearest to the approaching traffic.</p>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> Except at roundabouts and channelized right-turn lanes, where there is a marked crosswalk at the intersection, the YIELD sign should be installed in advance of the edge of the crosswalk that is nearest to the approaching traffic.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> Where two roads intersect at an acute angle, the STOP or YIELD sign should be positioned at an angle, or shielded, so that the legend is out of view of traffic to which it does not apply.</p>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> If a raised splitter island is available on the left-hand side of a multi-lane roundabout approach, an additional YIELD sign should be placed on the left-hand side of the approach.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> If a raised splitter island is available on the left-hand side of a single-lane roundabout approach, an additional YIELD sign may be placed on the left-hand side of the approach.</p>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> At wide-throat intersections or where two or more approach lanes of traffic exist on the signed approach, an additional STOP or YIELD sign may be installed on the left-hand side of the road and/or a stop or yield line may be used to improve observance of the right-of-way control. At channelized intersections or at divided roadways separated by a median or divisional island, the additional STOP or YIELD sign may be placed on a channelizing island, or in the median or on the divisional island. An additional STOP or YIELD sign may also be placed overhead facing the approach at the intersection to improve observance of the right- of-way control.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">18</span> More than one STOP sign or more than one YIELD sign shall not be placed on the same support facing in the same direction.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">19</span> For a yield-controlled channelized right-turn movement onto a roadway without an acceleration lane and for an entrance ramp onto a freeway or expressway without an acceleration lane, a NO MERGE AREA (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w4-5ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W4-5aP.svg">W4-5aP</a>) supplemental plaque (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c45">Section 2C.45</a>) may be mounted below a Yield Ahead (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w3-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W3-2.svg">W3-2</a>) sign and/or below a YIELD (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-2.svg">R1-2</a>) sign when engineering judgment indicates that road users would expect an acceleration lane to be present.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b19" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.19 Yield Here To Pedestrians Signs and Stop Here For Pedestrians Signs (R1-5 Series)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.19 &middot; manual page 165 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.19/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5.svg">R1-5</a> series signs are intended to mitigate the scenario that can place pedestrians at risk by blocking other drivers’ view of pedestrians and by blocking the pedestrians' view of the vehicles approaching in the adjacent lanes.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5.svg">R1-5</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5a.svg">R1-5a</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5b.svg">R1-5b</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5c.svg">R1-5c</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5d.svg">R1-5d</a>, and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5e.svg">R1-5e</a>) signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-2">Figure 2B-2</a>) shall be used if yield (stop) lines are used in advance of a marked crosswalk only where it crosses an uncontrolled multi-lane approach. The Stop Here for Pedestrians signs shall only be used where the law specifically requires that a driver must stop for a pedestrian in a crosswalk. The legend STATE LAW shall not be displayed on the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5.svg">R1-5</a> series signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02a</span> <span class="ca">The Stop Here for Pedestrian signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5b.svg">R1-5b</a>, <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5c.svg">R1-5c</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5e.svg">R1-5e</a>) are deleted as a stop is not required in California per   CVC § 21950.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> If yield (stop) lines and Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs are used in advance of a crosswalk that crosses an uncontrolled multi-lane approach, the signs should be placed 20 to 50 feet in advance of the nearest edge of the crosswalk (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/3b/#3b19">Section 3B.19</a> and <a href="#fig-3b-16">Figure 3B-16</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> When used with a School Crossing assembly within school zones (see Part 7), the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5a.svg">R1-5a</a> and R1- 5c signs shall be used in place of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5.svg">R1-5</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5b.svg">R1-5b</a> signs in accordance with Paragraph 2 of this Section.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> When used with a Trail Crossing assembly (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c54">Section 2C.54</a>), the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5d.svg">R1-5d</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5e.svg">R1-5e</a> signs shall be used in place of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5.svg">R1-5</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-5b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-5b.svg">R1-5b</a> signs in accordance with Paragraph 2 of this Section.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> When Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs are provided in advance of a crosswalk across an multi-lane approach, parking should be prohibited in the area between the yield (stop) line and the crosswalk.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Yield (stop) lines and Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs should not be used in advance of crosswalks that cross an approach to or departure from a roundabout.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs may be used in accordance with Paragraphs 2 through 4 of this Section even if yield (stop) lines are not used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> A Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-2.svg">W11-2</a>) warning sign may be placed overhead or may be post-mounted with a diagonal downward-pointing arrow (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w16-7p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W16-7P.svg">W16-7P</a>) plaque at the crosswalk location where Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs have been installed in advance of the crosswalk.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> If a <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-2.svg">W11-2</a> sign is post-mounted at the crosswalk location where a Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians sign is used on the approach, the Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians sign shall not be placed on the same post as the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-2.svg">W11-2</a> sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> An advance Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-2.svg">W11-2</a>) warning sign with an AHEAD or a distance supplemental plaque may be used in conjunction with a Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians sign on the approach to the same crosswalk.</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> In-Street Pedestrian Crossing signs and Yield Here to (Stop Here for) Pedestrians signs may be used together at the same crosswalk.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b20" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.20 In-Street and Overhead Pedestrian and Trail Crossing Signs (R1-6 and R1-9 Series)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.20 &middot; manual page 166 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.20/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The In-Street Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6.svg">R1-6</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6a.svg">R1-6a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-2">Figure 2B-2</a>), In-Street Trail Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6d.svg">R1-6d</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6e.svg">R1-6e</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-2">Figure 2B-2</a>), the Overhead Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9.svg">R1-9</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9a.svg">R1-9a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-2">Figure 2B-2</a>), or the Overhead Trail Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9d.svg">R1-9d</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9e.svg">R1-9e</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-2">Figure 2B-2</a>) may be used to remind road users of laws regarding right-of-way at an unsignalized crosswalk. The legend STATE LAW may be displayed at the top of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6.svg">R1-6</a> series and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9.svg">R1-9</a> series signs if applicable. On the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6.svg">R1-6</a> series signs, the legends STOP or YIELD may be used instead of the appropriate STOP sign or YIELD sign symbol.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Highway agencies may develop and apply criteria for determining the applicability of In-Street Pedestrian Crossing signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02a</span> <span class="ca">The In-Street Pedestrian crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6a.svg">R1-6a</a>), In-Street Trail Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6e.svg">R1-6e</a>), and Overhead Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9a.svg">R1-9a</a>), and  Overhead Trail Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9e.svg">R1-9e</a>) signs are deleted as a stop is not required in California per CVC § 21950.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The STOP FOR legend shall only be used in States where the State law specifically requires that a driver must stop for a pedestrian or a bicyclist in a crosswalk.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> If used, In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing signs shall only be placed in the roadway at the crosswalk location on the center line, on a median island, on a lane line, or on an edge line.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign shall not be post-mounted on the left-hand or right-hand side of the roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/3i/#3i02">Section 3I.02</a> contains information about the use of tubular markers to provide additional emphasis for a pedestrian crossing.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> If used, the Overhead Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign shall be placed over the roadway at the crosswalk location.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> When used at an uncontrolled crossing, the In-Street or Overhead Pedestrian Crossing sign shall be used only as a supplement to a Pedestrian Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-2.svg">W11-2</a>) warning sign with a diagonal downward-pointing arrow (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w16-7p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W16-7P.svg">W16-7P</a>) plaque at the crosswalk location.</p>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> When used at an uncontrolled crossing, the In-Street or Overhead Trail Crossing sign shall be used only as a supplement to a Trail Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w11-15/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W11-15.svg">W11-15</a>) warning sign with a diagonal downward- pointing arrow (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w16-7p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W16-7P.svg">W16-7P</a>) plaque at the crosswalk location.</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> An In-Street or Overhead Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign shall not be placed in advance of the crosswalk to educate road users about the State law prior to reaching the crosswalk, nor shall it be installed as an educational display that is not near any crosswalk.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> If an island (see Chapter 3J) is available, the In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign, if used, should be placed on the island.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing signs may be mounted back-to-back in the median or on the center line of an undivided roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> The In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign and the Overhead Pedestrian Crossing or Trail sign shall not be used at crosswalks on approaches controlled by a <span class="ca">stop control,</span> traffic control signal, pedestrian hybrid beacon, or an emergency-vehicle hybrid beacon.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> Except where the In-Street Crossing sign is placed on a physical island, the sign support shall be designed to bend over and then bounce back to its normal vertical position when struck by a vehicle.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> The In-Street and Overhead Pedestrian and Trail Crossing sign may be used at intersections or midblock pedestrian crossings with flashing beacons.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> The provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a15">Section 2A.15</a> concerning mounting height are not applicable for the In-Street Pedestrian Crossing sign. <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a18">Section 2A.18</a> contains information about sign mounting methods.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> The top of an In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign shall be a maximum of 4 feet above the pavement surface. The top of an In-Street Pedestrian or Trail Crossing sign placed in an island shall be a maximum of 4 feet above the island surface.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">18</span> The In-Street Pedestrian Crossing or Trail Crossing signs may be used seasonally to prevent damage in winter because of plowing operations, and may be removed at night if the pedestrian activity at night is minimal.</p>
<p><span class="pn">19</span> Both sign mounting types, In-Street Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-6.svg">R1-6</a> series) signs and Overhead Crossing (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r1-9/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R1-9.svg">R1-9</a> series) signs, may be used together at the same crosswalk.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Speed Limit Signs And Plaques</div>


<span id="2b21" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.21 Speed Limit Sign (R2-1)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.21 &middot; manual page 168 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.21/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> In general, the maximum speed limits applicable to rural and urban roads are established: A. Statutorily – a maximum speed limit applicable to a particular class of road, such as freeways or city streets, that is established by State law; or B. As speed zones – based on engineering studies.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> State statutory limits might restrict the maximum speed limit that can be established on a particular road, notwithstanding what an engineering study might indicate.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Agencies with designated authorities to set speed limits, which include States, and sometimes local jurisdictions, can establish non-statutory speed limits or designate reduced speed zones using an engineering study. Setting appropriate speed limits is especially important to ensure safety for all road users in varying types of contexts, particularly on roadways where adjacent land use suggests that trips could be served by varied modes. These situations include urban and suburban non-freeway arterials or rural arterials that serve as main streets in smaller communities, consistent with the context classifications of urban core, urban, suburban, and rural towns found in “A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets,” 2018 Edition, AASHTO. When setting a speed limit, a range of factors such as land-use context, pedestrian and bicyclist activity, crash history, intersection spacing, driveway density, roadway geometry, roadside conditions, roadway functional classification, traffic volume, and observed speeds can influence the speed limit determined in the engineering study. The engineering study will determine which of the recommended factors will prevail in setting the speed limit.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Jurisdictions can use speed limit setting tools and methods such as expert systems and those consistent with the safe system approach as part of the required engineering study for a non-statutory speed limit. As speed limit setting tools vary, jurisdictions need to be aware of their limitations and advantages, possible variation between the tools and the need to explore gaps or weaknesses of tools, and weigh the output accordingly in consideration of setting speed limits.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04a</span> <span class="ca">The setting of speed limits can be controversial and requires a rational and defensible determination to maintain public  confidence. Speed limits are normally set near the 85th-percentile speed that statistically represents one standard deviation above  the average speed and establishes the upper limit of what is considered reasonable and prudent. As with most laws, speed limits  need to depend on the voluntary compliance of the greater majority of motorists. Speed limits cannot be set arbitrarily low, as this  would create violators of the majority of drivers and would not command the respect of the public. Artificially low speed limits can  lead to poor compliance as well as large variations in speed within the traffic stream. Increased speed variance can also create more  conflicts and passing maneuvers.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> To achieve desired operating speeds, agencies often implement other speed management strategies concurrently with setting speed limits, such as traffic calming measures, geometric design features, speed safety cameras, and increased enforcement.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05a</span> <span class="ca">The most effective way to reduce speeds is through a combination of strategies using traffic control devices related to speed  management, roadway design and engineering solutions, traffic calming techniques and measures, public education, and  enforcement efforts. Effectively managing road user speed relies on numerous factors, which include enforcement, roadway  characteristics, surrounding environment, adjacent land use, and traffic control devices. Many studies find that engineering changes,  such as change a road’s infrastructure, are one of the most important factors in reducing vehicle operating speeds. Engineering  changes are also one of the most effective interventions at reducing pedestrian injury and fatality rates. Potential street engineering  changes, such as curb extensions, median islands, raised crosswalks, roundabouts, and speed bumps or speed humps, naturally  result in lower speeds. It is realized that these engineering changes can be costly and time-consuming to implement.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Speed zones (other than statutory speed limits) shall only be established on the basis of an engineering <span class="ca">and  traffic survey (E&amp;TS), also noted as an engineering</span> study that has been performed in accordance with traffic engineering practices. The engineering study shall consider the roadway context.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The engineering study should consider the roadway context. Among the factors that should be considered when conducting an engineering study for establishing or reevaluating speed limits within speed zones are the following: A.Roadway environment <span class="ca">not readily apparent to the driver (CVC § 627)</span> (such as roadside development, number and frequency of driveways and access points, and land use), functional classification, public transit volume and location or frequency of stops, parking practices, and pedestrian and bicycle facilities and activity; B.Roadway characteristics <span class="ca">not readily apparent to the driver (CVC § 627) </span>(such as lane widths, shoulder condition, grade, alignment, median type, and sight distance); C.Geographic context (such as an urban district, rural town center, non-urbanized rural area, or suburban area <span class="ca">residential district, business district, business activity district</span>), and multi-modal trip generation; D.Reported crash experience for at least a 12 <span class="ca">36</span>-month period; E.Speed distribution of free-flowing vehicles including the pace, median (50th-percentile), and 85th- precentile <span class="ca">percentile</span> speeds; and F.A review of past speed studies to identify any trends in operating speeds<span class="ca"> (CVC § 22358.8) </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">07a</span> <span class="ca">Refer to FHWA’s List of Known Errors for error in Paragraph 7, Item E text. Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/1a/#1a04">Section 1A.04</a> for more details.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> When the 85th-percentile speed is appreciably greater than the posted speed limit, and the roadway context does not support setting a higher speed limit, the engineering <span class="ca">(not E&amp;TS) </span>study should consider whether changesto geometric features, enforcement, and/or other speed-reduction countermeasures might improve compliance with the posted speed limit. A similar approach should be used if the results of past speed studies indicate that the 85th-percentile speed has consistently increased.</p>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> On urban and suburban arterials, and on rural arterials that serve as main streets through developed areas of communities, the 85th-percentile speed should not be used to set speed limits without consideration of all factors described in Paragraph 7 of this Section.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> On a freeway, expressway, or rural highway (outside urbanized locations or conditions),<span class="ca"> On a highway, </span>the speed limit that is posted within a speed zone should <span class="ca">shall</span> be within 5 mph <span class="ca">12.4 mph</span> of the 85th-percentile speed of free- flowing motor-vehicle traffic under the following conditions: A.All factors described in Paragraph 7 of this Section have been considered and determined to be non- mitigating. B.The measures described in Paragraph 8 of this Section have been considered to the extent practicable.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> State and local agencies should conduct engineering studies to reevaluate non-statutory speed limits on segments of their roadways that have undergone significant changes since the last review (such as changes to roadway context, the addition or elimination of parking or driveways, changes in the number of travel lanes, changes in the configuration of bicycle lanes, changes to road geometrics, changes in traffic control signal coordination, or significant changes in traffic volumes).</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> Speed studies for signalized intersection approaches should be taken outside the influence area of the traffic control signal, which is generally considered to be approximately ½ mile, to avoid obtaining skewed results for the speed distribution. If the signal spacing is less than 1 mile, the speed study should be at approximately the middle of the segment.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">12a</span> <span class="ca">Speed limits in California are governed by the CVC, §§ 22348 through 22413; also, pertinent sections are found in §§ 627 and  40802 and others referenced in this section. Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/1a/#1a05">Section 1A.05</a> for information regarding this publication.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">12b</span> <span class="ca">Refer to Part 6, <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/6b/#6b01">Section 6B.01</a> for speed limit signs in temporary traffic control zones. Refer to Part 7 for speed limit signs in school  areas. Basic Speed Law and Prima Facie Speed Limits – Refer to CVC §§ 22350 &amp; 22352</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">12c</span> <span class="ca">The basic speed law states “No person shall drive a vehicle upon a highway at a speed greater than is reasonable or prudent  having due regard for weather, visibility, the traffic on, and the surface and width of, the highway, and in no event at a speed which  endangers the safety of persons or property.”</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">12d</span> <span class="ca">Prima facie speed limits are specific limits and shall apply unless changed based upon an E&amp;TS and signs are posted  that display the new speed limit.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12e</span> <span class="ca">Prima facie speed limits may be preempted by the basic speed law, when roadway, traffic or weather conditions warrant a lower  speed.  Legal Authority for Establishing Speed Limits</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">12f</span> <span class="ca">Delegation of legal authority to set speed limits based on valid E&amp;TS on State highways is given to Caltrans District Directors.  The District Director of each transportation district is authorized to issue orders regulating the speed of traffic, up to 70 mph on State  highways. The Director of Caltrans retains the authority to approve variable, and minimum speed limits on State freeways.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">12g</span> <span class="ca">The speed limits shown in <a href="#fig-2b-101ca">Table 2B-101(CA)</a> shall apply, unless changed upon the basis of an E&amp;TS.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12h</span> <span class="ca">The speed limits shown in <a href="#fig-2b-102ca">Table 2B-102(CA)</a> may apply, unless changed upon E&amp;TS.  Variable Speed Limits on Freeways - Refer to CVC § 22355</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">12i</span> <span class="ca">The following speed limits may apply:  •</span><span class="ca"> Whenever Caltrans determines based upon an E&amp;TS that the safe and orderly movement of traffic upon any freeway  segment will be facilitated by the establishment of variable speed limits  •</span><span class="ca"> Caltrans may erect, regulate, and control signs upon the state highway which is a freeway, or any portion thereof, which, if  used, signs shall be designed to permit display of different speeds at various times of the day or night.  •</span><span class="ca"> Such signs need not conform to the standards &amp; specifications per CVC § 21400, but if used, shall be of sufficient size and  clarity to give adequate notice of the applicable speed limit.  Minimum Speed Limits on State Highways - Refer to CVC § 22400 </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">12j</span> <span class="ca">The following speed limits may apply:  • Whenever Caltrans determines based upon an E&amp;TS that slow speeds on any part of a state highway consistently impede  the normal and reasonable movement of traffic, Caltrans may determine and declare a minimum speed limit. Appropriate  signs giving notice shall then be installed on that segment.  •</span><span class="ca"> A motorist can be cited for stopping or impeding the normal and reasonable movement of traffic unless the stop is  necessary for safe operation and in compliance with the law.  Engineering and Traffic Survey (E&amp;TS) </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">12k</span> <span class="ca">CVC § 627 defines the term “Engineering and traffic survey” and lists its requirements.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">12l</span> <span class="ca">An E&amp;TS shall include, among other requirements deemed necessary by Caltrans, consideration of all of the following:  A.</span><span class="ca">Prevailing speeds as determined by traffic engineering measurements.
