B.</span><span class="ca">Collision records.  C.</span><span class="ca">Highway, traffic, and roadside conditions not readily apparent to the driver. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">12m</span> <span class="ca">The E&amp;TS should contain sufficient information to document that the required three items of CVC § 627 are provided and that  other conditions not readily apparent to a driver are properly identified.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">12n</span> <span class="ca">Prevailing speeds are determined by a speed zone survey. A speed zone survey should include:  A.</span><span class="ca"> The intent of the speed measurements is to determine the actual speed of unimpeded traffic. The speed of traffic should not  be altered by concentrated law enforcement, or other means, just prior to, or while taking the speed measurements.  B.</span><span class="ca">Speeds should be read directly from a radar or other electronic speed measuring devices; or,  C.</span><span class="ca">Devices, other than radar, capable of accurately distinguishing and measuring the unimpeded speed of free flowing vehicles  may be used.  D.</span><span class="ca">A location should be selected where prevailing speeds are representative of the entire speed zone section. If speeds vary  on a given route, more than one speed zone section may be required, with separate measurements for each section.  Locations for measurements should be chosen so as to minimize the effects of traffic signals or stop signs.  E.</span><span class="ca"> Speed measurements should be taken during off-peak hours between peak traffic periods on weekdays. If there is difficulty  in obtaining the desired quantity, speed measurements may be taken during any period with free flowing traffic.  F.</span><span class="ca"> The weather should be fair (dry pavement) with no unusual conditions prevailing.  G.</span><span class="ca">The surveyor and equipment should not affect the traffic speeds. For this reason, an unmarked car is recommended, and  the radar speed meter located as inconspicuously as possible.  H.</span><span class="ca">In order for the sample to be representative of the actual traffic flow, the minimum sample should be 100 vehicles in each  survey. In no case should the sample contain less than 50 vehicles.  I.</span><span class="ca"> Short speed zones of less than 0.5 miles should be avoided, except in transition areas.  J.</span><span class="ca">Speed zone changes should be coordinated with changes in roadway conditions or roadside development.  K.</span><span class="ca">Speed zoning should be coordinated with adjacent jurisdictions. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">12o</span> <span class="ca">Physical conditions such as width, curvature, grade and surface conditions, or any other condition readily apparent to the driver,  in the absence of other factors, would not require special downward speed zoning. Refer to CVC § 22358.5.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12p</span> <span class="ca">When qualifying an appropriate speed limit, local authorities may also consider all of the following findings:  A.</span><span class="ca">Residential density, if any of the following conditions exist on the particular portion of highway and the property contiguous  thereto, other than a business district:  1.</span><span class="ca">Upon one side of the highway, within 0.25 miles, the contiguous property fronting thereon is occupied by 13 or more  separate dwelling houses or business structures.  2.</span><span class="ca">Upon both sides of the highway, collectively, within a distance of 0.25 miles the contiguous property fronting thereon is  occupied by 16 or more separate dwelling houses or business structures.  3.</span><span class="ca">The portion of highway is larger than 0.25 miles but has the ratio of separate dwelling houses or business structures to  the length of the highway described in either subparagraph 1 or 2 above.  B.</span><span class="ca">Safety of bicyclists and pedestrians, with increased consideration for vulnerable pedestrian groups including children,  seniors, persons with disabilities, users of personal assistive mobility devices, and the unhoused. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">12q</span> <span class="ca">The following two forms may be used to document speed zoning based on an E&amp;TS to establish speed limits:  1. State Highways - The E&amp;TS for State highways is made under the direction of the Caltrans District traffic engineer. Long  form should be used to document the data and reports including:  a.</span><span class="ca"> One copy of Speed Zone Survey Sheet (Refer to <a href="#fig-2b-101ca">Figure 2B-101(CA)</a>) showing:  • A north arrow  • Name and/or highway number of the route to be zoned  • Post Mile or other distance measurement  • Limits of the proposed zones for each direction of travel  • Roads and crossroad names, number and width of lanes, indicate parking restrictions  • Appropriate notations showing type of roadside development, such as “scattered business,” “solid residential,” etc.  Schools adjacent to the highway are shown, but other buildings need not be plotted unless they are a factor in the  speed recommendation or the point of termination of a speed zone.  • Collision rates for the zones involved  • Annual average daily traffic volume  • Location of traffic signals, signs and markings  • If the highway is divided, the limits of zones for each direction of travel  • Plotted 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile and pace speeds at location taken showing speed profile  b.</span><span class="ca"> A report to the District Director that includes:  •</span><span class="ca"> The reason for the initiation of speed zone survey.  •</span><span class="ca"> Recommendations and supporting reasons.  •</span><span class="ca"> The enforcement jurisdictions involved and the recommendations and opinions of those officials.  •</span><span class="ca"> The stationing or reference post in mileage at the beginning and ending of each proposed zone and any  intermediate equations. Location ties must be given to readily identifiable physical features.  2. Roadways under the jurisdiction of City or County:  a.</span><span class="ca"> Short form may be used for documenting speed zoning (<a href="#fig-2b-104ca">Figure 2B-104(CA)</a>).  b.</span><span class="ca"> Determination of Speed Limits - Figures 2B-103(CA) &amp; 2B-104(CA) show examples of data sheets which may be used  to record speed observations. Specific types of vehicles may be tallied by use of letter symbols in appropriate squares. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">12r</span> <span class="ca">In most situations, the short form for local streets and roads will be adequate; however, the procedure or long form used on  State highways may be used at the option of the local agency.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">12s</span> <span class="ca">The establishment of a speed limit of more than 5 mph below the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed should be done with great care as  studies have shown that establishing a speed limit less than the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile generally results in an increase in collision rates; in  addition, this may make violators of a disproportionate number of the reasonable majority of drivers. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">12t</span> <span class="ca">Generally, the most decisive evidence of conditions not readily apparent to the driver are identified</span><span class="ca">in collision histories. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">12u</span> <span class="ca">Speed limits are established at or near the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed, which is defined as that speed at or below which 85 percent of  the traffic is moving. The 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed is often referred to as the critical speed. Pace speed is defined as the 10 mph  increment of speed containing the largest number of vehicles (Refer to <a href="#fig-2b-102ca">Figure 2B-102(CA)</a>). The lower limit of the pace is plotted on  the Speed Zone Survey Sheets as an aid in determining the proper zone limits. Speed limits higher than the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed are  not generally considered reasonable and prudent. Speed limits below the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed does not ordinarily facilitate the  orderly movement of traffic and require constant enforcement to maintain compliance. Speed limits established on the basis of the  85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed conform to the consensus of those who drive highways as to what speed is reasonable and prudent, and are  not dependent on the judgment of one or a few individuals. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">12v</span> <span class="ca">The majority of drivers comply with the basic speed law. Speed limits set at or near the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed provide law  enforcement officers with a limit to cite drivers who will not conform to what the majority considers reasonable and prudent. Further  studies show that establishing a speed limit at less than the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile (Critical Speed) generally results in an increase in  collision rates. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12w</span> <span class="ca">When roadside development results in traffic conflicts and unusual conditions which are not readily apparent to drivers, as  indicated in collision records, speed limits somewhat below the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile may be justified. Concurrence and support of  enforcement officials are necessary for the successful operation of a speed zone. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">12x</span> <span class="ca">Speed zones of less than 0.5 miles in rural locations, 0.25 miles for urban locations, and short transition zones should be  avoided.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">12y</span> <span class="ca">Any existing E&amp;TS that was performed before January 1, 2022 in accordance with previous traffic control device standards is not  required to be updated until it is due for reevaluation per the 5, 7 or 14 year criteria. An E&amp;TS can originally exist for 7 years and may  be extended to 14 years if the conditions are met.  CVC § 22358.6 – 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca">-Percentile speed, Rounding, 5 mph Increment, 5 mph speed reduction and Maximum Speed Reduction </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">12z</span> <span class="ca">When a speed limit is to be posted, it shall be established at the nearest 5 mph increment of the 85th-percentile speed  of free-flowing traffic (CVC § 22358.6(a)), except as shown in the two Options described in paragraphs 12aa and 12ab, or if  using additional 5 mph speed reduction for safety corridor designation (CVC § 22358.7(a)(1)) or adjacent to land or facility  generating high concentrations of bicyclists and pedestrians (CVC § 22358.7(a)(2)).</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">12aa  For cases in which the nearest 5 mph increment of the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca">-percentile speed would require a rounding down, the speed limit may  be reduced by 5 mph from the nearest 5 mph increment of the 85th-percentile speed, in compliance with CVC §§ 627 and 22358.5.  CVC §§ 22353, 22353.2, 22353.3, 22353.4, and 22353.5, may also be considered, if applicable. Refer to Standard below for  documentation requirements. Refer to CVC § 22358.6(b).  12ab  For cases in which rounding the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca">-percentile speed to the nearest 5 mph increment would require a rounding up, the speed  limit may be rounded down to the nearest 5 mph increment below the 85th percentile speed. Refer to CVC § 21400(b). Refer to CVC  § 22358.6(c). </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12ac</span><span class="ca">If 5 mph reduction is applied to the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed after it is rounded to the nearest 5 mph increment and the  required rounding is a rounding down, to establish posted speed limit, an E&amp;TS shall document in writing the conditions  and justification for the 5 mph reduction and be approved by a registered Civil or Traffic Engineer. The reasons for the  lower speed limit shall be in compliance with CVC §§ 627 and 22358.5. Refer to § 22358.6(b).  12ad  If the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed is rounded down to the lower 5 mph increment, in cases in which the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed  needs to round up to the nearest 5 mph increment, to establish posted speed limit, the speed limit shall not be reduced any  further pursuant to CVC § 22358.6(b).  12ae</span><span class="ca">The total reduction from the 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca"> percentile speed shall not exceed 12.4 mph. Refer to CVC § 22358.6(e). </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="ca">12af Refer to <a href="#fig-2b-103ca">Table 2B-103(CA)</a>, which provides examples of 85</span><span class="ca">th</span><span class="ca">-percentile speed values and the application of the speed limit  policies and criteria applicable per CVC §§ 22358.6 and 22358.7.  CVC § 22358.7 – Safety corridor and Land or Facilities Generating High Concentrations of Bicyclists and Pedestrians </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12ag</span><span class="ca">Additional lowering of the speed limits from standards, as included in paragraph 12z and Options as described in  paragraphs 12aa, and 12ab, is prohibited, except for the roadway segments designated as “safety corridor” or “land or  facilities that generate high concentrations of bicyclists and pedestrians” in compliance with CVC §§ 22358.6(d) and  22358.7. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">12ah</span><span class="ca"> Caltrans or local authority may additionally lower the speed limits by 5 mph from the final value determined based on paragraphs  12z, 12aa and 12ab if, after completing an E&amp;TS, it finds that the speed limit is still more than is reasonable or safe, for either of the  following reasons:  1.</span><span class="ca">The portion of a highway has been designated as a safety corridor.  2.</span><span class="ca">The portion of highway meets the definition of land or facility that generates high concentrations of bicyclists or pedestrians.  CVC § 22358.7(a)(1) – “Safety Corridor” Definition </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12ai A safety corridor shall be defined as a roadway segment within an overall roadway network where the highest number  of serious injury and fatality crashes occur.  12aj One or more crash weighting factors as provided in paragraph 12ak of this section shall be used to prioritize the  locations of fatal and serious injury crashes in developing the “Safety Corridor”. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">12ak</span><span class="ca"> The crash weighting factors to prioritize the locations of fatal and serious injury crashes in developing the "Safety Corridor" may  include, but are not limited to, the factors included in <a href="#fig-2b-104ca">Table 2B-104(CA)</a>.  12al Data used to determine a safety corridor may be from the most recent E&amp;TS performed. The crash data source may include, but  is not limited to, California Highway Patrol’s (CHP) Statewide Integrated Traffic Records System (SWITRS). </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12am </span><span class="ca">The prioritized subset of safety corridors shall:  1.</span><span class="ca">Identify specific locations with high crash occurrences.  2.</span><span class="ca">Identify corridor-level segments with a pattern of crash reoccurrence.  3.</span><span class="ca">Be able to be stratified by mode. 12an</span><span class="ca">Safety corridors shall represent a prioritized subset of the overall roadway network within an authority’s  responsibilities and shall not exceed one-fifth of the overall roadway network. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="ca">12ao</span><span class="ca">A jurisdiction should use three to five years of the most recent crash data to determine a safety corridor based on Fatal and  Serious Injury data. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">12ap</span><span class="ca"> For crash coverage, safety corridors may identify the subset of the overall roadway network where a minimum of 25% of the  Fatal + Serious Injury (F+SI) crashes occur.  12aq </span><span class="ca">To identify logical termini, the geographic extent of a safety corridor may be determined by non-engineering staff. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12ar</span><span class="ca">A licensed professional engineer shall sign off on logical termini identified for a safety corridor using existing E&amp;TS.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">12as</span><span class="ca"> Crash/Volume rate may be used to provide additional locations to be included in the safety corridor. Local agencies may use  proactive measures as indicators.  CVC § 22358.7(a)(2) – “Land or facility that generates high concentrations of bicyclists or pedestrians” definition </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12at A land or facility that generates high concentrations of bicyclists or pedestrians shall be defined as the portion of the  highway where one or more of any of the generators listed in <a href="#fig-2b-105ca">Table 2B-105(CA)</a> are present within a distance of 1320 feet  longitudinally of highway, and meets condition specified in paragraph 12au.  12au  Crash data demonstrates the highway segment is within the top twenty percent of pedestrian and/or bicyclist fatalities  or serious injuries over a three-to-five-year. 12av</span><span class="ca">A highway segment shall be defined as the portion of the highway where a location that meets the aforementioned  criteria is present within a distance of 1320 feet longitudinally of highway. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">12aw</span><span class="ca"> A highway segment may be longer than 1320 feet longitudinally of highway provided that a minimum of one location within the  top twenty percent of fatal and serious injury pedestrian and/or bicyclist crashes within a three-to-five-year period is present for every  1320 feet. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12ax</span><span class="ca">The top twenty percent of pedestrian and/or bicyclist fatalities or serious injury crashes within a three to five year  period shall be based on the geographic area within the jurisdiction.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">12ay</span><span class="ca"> A high concentration of pedestrians and bicyclists may be longer than 1320 feet longitudinally of highway provided that a  minimum of one generator is present for every 1320 feet.  12az</span><span class="ca"> Data used to determine high concentration locations may be obtained from the most recently performed E&amp;TS.  CVC § 22358.8 (Retain currently adopted or restore immediately prior speed limit)  12ba </span><span class="ca">Caltrans or a local authority may retain the currently adopted speed limit without further reduction or restore the immediately  prior adopted speed limit on a highway that is not a freeway without further reduction as provided in CVC § 22358.8.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bb</span><span class="ca">Currently adopted speed limit or immediately prior adopted speed limit shall only be retained, by Caltrans Director’s  Order for SHS or local ordinance for highways under local jurisdiction, if after completing an E&amp;TS, Caltrans or a local  authority finds that the speed limit is still more than reasonable or safe, and that speed limit was established with an E&amp;TS  and if a registered engineer has evaluated the section of highway and determined that no additional general purpose lanes  have been added to the roadway since completion of the traffic survey that established the prior speed limit. 12bc</span><span class="ca"> If Caltrans or a local authority decides to use lower speed limit based on CVC § 22358.8, after completing an E&amp;TS and  finding that the speed limit is still more than is reasonable or safe, it shall not be reduced by any more than 5 mph from the  currently adopted speed limit nor below the immediately prior speed limit. Refer to CVC § 22358.8(b).  12bd CVC § 22358.8 shall not be applicable to a roadway that is a freeway.  CVC § 22358.9 – Business Activity District</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">12be</span><span class="ca"> Caltrans or a local authority may, by Caltrans Director’s Order for SHS or local ordinance for highways under local jurisdiction,  determine and declare a 25 or 20 mph prima facie speed limit on a highway that is not a freeway and that is contiguous to a business  activity district when posted with a sign that indicates a speed limit of 25 or 20 mph if the highway segment meets all of the following  conditions:  1.</span><span class="ca">A maximum of four traffic lanes.  2.</span><span class="ca">A maximum posted 30 mph prima facie speed limit immediately prior to and after the business activity district, if establishing  a 25 mph speed limit.  3.</span><span class="ca">A maximum posted 25 mph prima facie speed limit immediately prior to and after the business activity district, if establishing  a 20 mph speed limit.  12bf A “business activity district” is that portion of a highway that is not a freeway and the property contiguous thereto that includes  central or neighborhood downtowns, urban villages, or zoning designations that prioritize commercial land uses at the downtown or  neighborhood scale and meets a least three of the following four requirements:  4.</span><span class="ca">No less than 50 percent of the contiguous property fronting the highway consists of retail or dining commercial uses,  including outdoor dining, that open directly onto sidewalks adjacent to the highway.  5.</span><span class="ca">Parking, including parallel, diagonal, or perpendicular spaces located alongside the highway.  6.</span><span class="ca">Traffic control signals or stop signs regulating traffic flow on the highway, located at intervals of no more than 600 feet.  7.</span><span class="ca">Marked crosswalk(s) not controlled by a traffic control device. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bg</span><span class="ca">Caltrans or a local authority shall not declare a prima facie speed limit on a portion of a highway where it has already  lowered the speed limit as permitted for designated safety corridors (CVC § 22358.7) or using the land or facility adjacent to  high concentration of pedestrians and bicyclists (CVC § 22358.7) or retained the currently adopted speed limit   (CVC § 22358.8) or have restored the immediately prior adopted speed limit (CVC § 22358.8). Refer to CVC § 22358.9(c). 12bh CVC § 22358.9 shall not be applicable to a roadway that is a freeway. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bi CVC §§ 22358.7, 22358.8 and 22358.9 and their related policies are also applicable on any privately owned and maintained  roads or commercial establishments, if the private road or private property has been subjected to the CVC application by the private  property owner or a particular city or county enacts an ordinance or resolution to this effect. Refer to CVC §§ 21100, 21100.1, 21107,  21107.5, 21107.6, and 21107.7.  Truck Speed Zone on Descending Grades </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bj Highway descending grades, if used for posting TRUCK Speed Limit signs (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a> and M4-4P) for trucks travelling downhill,  should have recorded incident history of runaway commercial vehicles. Descending grades shorter than 1 mile should be avoided for  posting signs because deceleration of vehicles due to braking action can generally provide sufficient control on descending grades of  less than 1 mile. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bk</span><span class="ca"> To establish a downhill truck speed limit, a physical profile showing length and gradient and a downhill speed profile for three or  more axle commercial vehicles with a gross rating of 10,000 lbs. or more will be provided. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bl Speed profiles for truck speed limits shall be prepared on the same form as other speed surveys and remain valid for  up to 7 years and may be extended to 14 years if conditions are met. An analysis of collisions involving trucks shall be  prepared.  Speed Traps </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bm</span><span class="ca"> Refer to CVC § 40802 for Speed Traps. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bn</span><span class="ca">A speed trap shall not apply to a local street, road, school zone, senior zone, or business activity district. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bo</span><span class="ca"> Senior zone is an area approaching or passing a senior center building or other facility primarily used by senior citizens, or the  grounds thereof that is contiguous to a highway and on which is posted a standard “SENIOR” warning sign pursuant to   CVC § 22352.  12bp</span><span class="ca"> Business activity district is a section of highway described in CVC § 22358.9(b) in which a standard 25 mph or 20 mph speed  limit sign has been posted pursuant to CVC § 22358.9(a)(1).</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bq</span><span class="ca">A section of highway shall be defined as a speed trap if the prima facie speed limit is not justified by an E&amp;TS within  five years, and the enforcement of the speed limit involves the use of radar or any other electronic device that measures the  speed of moving objects, or seven years when using radar and all of the criteria specified in Paragraph 12bn are met.  12br This time provision shall be extended to seven years if the existing speed limit is initially valid for 5 years, when using  radar and all of the following criteria are met:  • The arresting officer has successfully completed a minimum of 24 hours of radar operator course. Approved and  certified by the Commission on Peace Officer Standards and Training.  • The radar used to measure the speed meets or exceeds the minimal operational standards of the National Traffic  Highway Safety Administration, and has been calibrated within three years of the alleged violation.  12bs  This time provision shall be extended to seven years if the existing speed limit is initially valid for 5 years, when using  laser or other electronic device (other than radar) and all of the following criteria are met:  • The arresting officer has successfully completed a minimum of 24 hours of radar operator course. Approved and  certified by the Commission on Peace Officer Standards and Training.  • The arresting officer has successfully completed a minimum of 2 hours of additional training. Course</span><span class="ca">approved  and certified by the Commission on Peace Officer Standards and Training .  • The laser or other electronic device used to measure the speed meets or exceeds the minimal operational  standards of the National Traffic Highway Safety Administration, and has been calibrated within three years of the  alleged violation. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">12bt This time provision for an E&amp;TS may be extended to fourteen years when all of the above conditions are met and no significant  changes in roadway or traffic conditions have occurred, including changes in adjoining property or land use, roadway width, or traffic  volume as determined by a registered engineer.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> The Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) shall display the limit established by law, ordinance, <span class="ca">resolution,</span> regulation, or as adopted by the authorized agency based on an engineering study. The speed limits displayed shall be in multiples of 5 mph.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) signs, indicating speed limits for which posting is required by law, shall be located at the points of change from one speed limit to another.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">14a</span> <span class="ca">Sections 2A.13 and 2A.16 provide guidance provisions on the location and lateral offset for signs.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">14b</span> <span class="ca">Where speed zones are longer than 1-mile, intermediate signs may be placed at approximate 1 mile intervals.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">14c</span> <span class="ca">For three or more lanes in each direction, dual installation on both the left-side and right-side of the roadway may be used.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">14d</span> <span class="ca">Freeways with a speed limit, 65 mph or 70 mph, Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) signs shall be posted as follows:  •</span><span class="ca"> At the beginning of the segment, on the right-hand side of the roadway.  •</span><span class="ca"> Subsequently throughout the segment, at a maximum of 10-mile interval and immediately after locations where  significant volumes of traffic enter the segment, on the right-hand side of the roadway. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">•</span><span class="ca"> The 10-mile interval may be modified to include locations following entrance ramps.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">14e</span> <span class="ca">Freeways segment where with a speed limit 55 mph, Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) signs shall be posted as follows:  • The beginning of the segment on the right-hand side of the roadway.  • Subsequently throughout the segment, at maximum 3-mile interval and immediately after locations where  significant volumes of traffic enter the segment with no more than 3 interchanges between signs, on the right-hand  side of the roadway.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">14f</span> <span class="ca">Expressways with a speed limit 55 mph or 65 mph, Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) signs shall be posted as follows:  • At the beginning of the segment, on the right-hand side of the roadway.  • Subsequently throughout the segment, at 3- to 5-mile intervals and immediately after locations where significant  volumes of traffic enter the segment, on the right-hand side of the roadway.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">14g</span> <span class="ca">Conventional highways with a speed limit 55 mph or 65 mph, Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) signs shall be posted as follows:  • At the beginning of the segment, on the right-hand side of the roadway.  • Subsequently throughout the segment, at maximum 5- to 10-mile intervals and immediately after locations where  significant volumes of traffic enter the segment, on the right-hand side of the roadway.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">14h</span> <span class="ca">Pavement markings with appropriate numerals (refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/3b/#3b20">Section 3B.20</a>) may be used to supplement speed limit signs.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> At the downstream end of the section to which a particular speed limit applies, a Speed Limit sign showing the next speed limit shall be installed.</p>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> Speed Limit signs indicating the statutory speed limits shall be installed at entrances to the State and, where appropriate, at jurisdictional boundaries in urban areas.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> Additional Speed Limit signs should be installed beyond interchanges and major intersections and at other locations where it is necessary to remind road users of the speed limit that is applicable.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">17a</span> <span class="ca">The Speed Zone Ahead (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r2-4ca.webp">R2-4(CA)</a>) sign shall always be followed by a Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign installed at the beginning  of the zone where the speed limit applies.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">17b</span> <span class="ca">The End Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r3ca.webp">R3(CA)</a>) signs, shall be placed at the end of the final speed zone, before the highway reverts to  maximum statutory speed limit.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">17c</span> <span class="ca">The End Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r3ca.webp">R3(CA)</a>) sign shall only be used to mark the end of a speed zone.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">17d</span> <span class="ca">The End Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r3ca.webp">R3(CA)</a>) sign shall not be used at a transition into a change in speed limits within a reduced zone.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">17e</span> <span class="ca">The End Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r3ca.webp">R3(CA)</a>) signs, shall be placed at the end of all speed zones and within a reasonable distance of  no greater than 1000 ft from the next Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">17f</span> <span class="ca">The Speed Zone Ahead (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r2-4ca.webp">R2-4(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-3ca">Figure 2B-3(CA)</a>) may be used to inform the motorist of a speed zone.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">17g</span> <span class="ca">The End Truck Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-14/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-14.svg">R2-14</a>) sign or the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r3ca.webp">R3(CA)</a> sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-3ca">Figure 2B-3(CA)</a>) with the TRUCK (M4-4P) plaque may be  used to mark the end of truck speed zones on descending grades.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">17h</span> <span class="ca">The SLOWER TRAFFIC KEEP RIGHT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r4-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R4-3.svg">R4-3</a>) signs may be installed at locations where there is a tendency of the motorists to  drive in the left-hand lane(s) below the normal speed of traffic.  Truck and Bus Speed Limit Signs   Standard</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">17i</span> <span class="ca">The TRUCKS, 3 AXLES OR MORE 55 (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-3bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r6-3bca.webp">R6-3B(CA)</a>) sign, the ALL VEHICLES WHEN TOWING 55 (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-4bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r6-4bca.webp">R6-4B(CA)</a>) sign, or the  Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign and the TRUCK SPEED LIMIT(<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2P.svg">R2-2P</a>) plaque, shall be placed to indicate the truck speed limit in all  truck speed zones.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">17j</span> <span class="ca">The Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign and the BUS SPEED LIMIT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2aP.svg">R2-2aP</a>(CA)) plaque shall be placed to indicate bus speed limit  in all bus speed zones.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">17k</span> <span class="ca">Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b22">Section 2B.22</a> for TRUCK and BUS speed zone signs.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">18</span> The “Traffic Control Devices Handbook” contains suggested criteria on the spacing of speed limit signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">18a</span> <span class="ca">Refer to FHWA’s List of Known Errors for error in Paragraph 18 text. Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/1a/#1a04">Section 1A.04</a> for more details.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">18b</span> <span class="ca">The Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) signs and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-3bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r6-3bca.webp">R6-3B(CA)</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-4bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r6-4bca.webp">R6-4B(CA)</a> signs should be placed in a location to be most effectively viewed  by the approaching motorists.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">18c</span> <span class="ca">When used, the TRUCKS, 3 AXLES OR MORE 55 (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-3bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r6-3bca.webp">R6-3B(CA)</a>) sign shall be installed approximately 750 feet following  each <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a> sign, both at the beginning and throughout each 60, 65 or 70 mph segment.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">18d</span> <span class="ca">The ALL VEHICLES WHEN TOWING 55 (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-4bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r6-4bca.webp">R6-4B(CA)</a>) sign shall be installed approximately 750 feet following the R6- 3B(CA) sign.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">18e</span> <span class="ca">The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-3bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r6-3bca.webp">R6-3B(CA)</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-4bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r6-4bca.webp">R6-4B(CA)</a> signs should be placed on highway segments where speeds in excess of 55 mph are permitted.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="ca">The existing AUTOS WITH TRAILERS, TRUCKS 55 MAXIMUM (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-1__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R6-1</a>(CA)) sign may remain in place until it is knocked down,  damaged, stolen, vandalized, or otherwise reaches the end of its useful life. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">18g</span> <span class="ca">The local California Highway Patrol office may be consulted to identify highway segments where enforcement is an issue. On  these segments early replacement of existing <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R6-1__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R6-1</a>(CA) signs may be necessary.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">18h</span> <span class="ca">Refer to CVC § 22406 for types of vehicles subject to the 55 mph maximum speed limit.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">19</span> If a jurisdiction has a policy of installing Speed Limit signs in accordance with statutory requirements only on the streets that enter a city, neighborhood, or residential area to indicate the speed limit that is applicable to the entire city, neighborhood, or residential area unless otherwise posted, a CITYWIDE (R2- 5aP), NEIGHBORHOOD (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-5bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-5bP.svg">R2-5bP</a>), or RESIDENTIAL (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-5cp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-5cP.svg">R2-5cP</a>) plaque may be mounted above the Speed Limit sign and an UNLESS OTHERWISE POSTED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-5p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-5P.svg">R2-5P</a>) plaque may be mounted below the Speed Limit sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">20</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c40">Section 2C.40</a> contains information about the use of speed zone signs to inform road users of a reduced or variable speed zone to provide advance notice to comply with the posted speed limit ahead.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">21</span> If a <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w3-5b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W3-5b.svg">W3-5b</a> sign is posted to provide notice of a variable speed zone, an END VARIABLE SPEED LIMIT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-13/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-13.svg">R2-13</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) may be installed at the downstream end of the zone to provide notice to road users of the termination of the speed zone.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">22</span> If a <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w3-5c/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W3-5c.svg">W3-5c</a> sign is posted to provide notice of a truck speed zone, an END TRUCK SPEED LIMIT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-14/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-14.svg">R2-14</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) shall be installed at the downstream end of the zone to provide notice to road users of the termination of the speed zone.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">23</span> An advisory speed plaque (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c59">Section 2C.59</a>) mounted below a warning sign should be used to warn road users of an advisory speed for a roadway condition. A Speed Limit sign should not be used for this purpose.</p>
<p><span class="pn">24</span> Advance traffic control warning signs (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c35">Section 2C.35</a>), intersection warning signs (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c41">Section 2C.41</a>), and/or other traffic control devices are appropriate warning prior to a signalized intersection. A Speed Limit sign should not be used for this purpose.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">25</span> Two types of Speed Limit signs may be used: one to designate passenger car speeds, including any nighttime information or maximum or minimum speed limit that might apply; and the other to show any special speed limits for trucks and other vehicles.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">26</span> No more than three speed limits should be displayed on any one Speed Limit sign or assembly. <span class="ca">Speed Enforced Signs </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">26a</span> <span class="ca">The SPEED ENFORCED BY RADAR (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r48ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r48ca.webp">R48(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-3ca">Figure 2B-3(CA)</a>) may be used where the California Highway  Patrol has received authority to use radar and requests such signs.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">26b</span> <span class="ca">One sign should be used in each direction at the beginning of the segment of roadway, and at intervening major route  intersections, where radar enforcement is in effect.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">26c</span> <span class="ca">The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r48ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r48ca.webp">R48(CA)</a> sign is a stand-alone sign intended to alert motorists that speed is enforced by radar on a particular segment of  roadway.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">26d</span> <span class="ca">The RADAR ENFORCED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r48-1pca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r48-1pca.webp">R48-1P(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-3ca">Figure 2B-3(CA)</a>) may be used in combination with the Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>)  sign on any roadway where law enforcement has the authority to use radar.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">26e</span> <span class="ca">When used, the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r48-1pca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r48-1pca.webp">R48-1P(CA)</a> sign should be placed below the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a> sign, at the beginning of the segment of roadway and at  intervening major intersections, where radar enforcement is in effect.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">26f</span> <span class="ca">The SPEED ENFORCED BY AIRCRAFT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r48-2ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r48-2ca.webp">R48-2(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-3ca">Figure 2B-3(CA)</a>) may be placed, when requested by the  California Highway Patrol, on sections of highway regularly patrolled by aircraft.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">26g</span> <span class="ca">The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r48-2ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r48-2ca.webp">R48-2(CA)</a> sign shall be used for both directions of travel.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">26h</span> <span class="ca">The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r48-2ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r48-2ca.webp">R48-2(CA)</a> sign should be placed at the beginning of the section and spaced at 25-mile intervals.  Speed Zones in Temporary Traffic Control Areas</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">26i</span> <span class="ca">For signing and establishing speed zones in temporary traffic control areas, refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/6b/#6b01">Section 6B.01</a> in Part 6.  Traffic Signals within Speed Zones</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">26j</span> <span class="ca">An agency changing the speed limits within its jurisdiction shall report the speed limit change to the agency operating  and maintaining traffic signals within the speed zone no later than 30 days before changing the posted speed limit.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">26k</span> <span class="ca">Changing the signal timing and adjusting the advance detector loops based on the revised speed limits can enhance the  operations of the traffic signal.  Use of Metric System Designations – Refer to CVC § 21351.3</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">26l</span> <span class="ca">Dual units for speed limits on signs may be placed on local streets and roads in both Metric and English units.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">26m</span> <span class="ca">If used, dual unit speed limits should be rounded to the nearest 10 km/h for Metric and 5 mph for English units for posting on  signs on local streets and roads.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">26n</span> <span class="ca">Refer to AASHTO’s Traffic Engineering Metric Conversion Factors. Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/1a/#1a05">Section 1A.05</a> for information regarding this  publication.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">26o</span> <span class="ca">Metric speed limits shall not be placed on State highways.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">27</span> A variable speed limit sign that changes the speed limit for traffic and ambient conditions may be installed provided that the appropriate speed limit is displayed at the proper times and locations in accordance with Paragraphs 9 and 10 of this Section.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">28</span> The variable speed limit sign legend “SPEED LIMIT” shall be a black legend on a white retroreflective background. The variable speed limit legend shall be displayed in white LEDs on an opaque black background.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">29</span> <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c13">Section 2C.13</a> contains information about the use of a Vehicle Speed Feedback plaque mounted below a Speed Limit sign that displays to approaching drivers the speed at which they are traveling.</p>
<p><span class="pn">30</span> Advisory speed signs and plaques are discussed in Sections 2C.12 and 2C.59. Temporary traffic control zone speed signs are discussed in Part 6. The WORK ZONE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/g20-5ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/G20-5aP.svg">G20-5aP</a>) plaque intended for installation above a Speed Limit sign is discussed in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/6g/#6g08">Section 6G.08</a>. School Speed Limit signs are discussed in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/7b/#7b05">Section 7B.05</a>.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b22" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.22 Vehicle Speed Limit Plaques (R2-2P Series)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.22 &middot; manual page 179 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.22/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Where a special speed limit applies to certain classes of vehicles, the Truck Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2P.svg">R2-2P</a>) plaque, Bus Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2aP.svg">R2-2aP</a>) plaque, Truck-Bus Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2bP.svg">R2-2bP</a>) plaque, or Vehicles over X Tons Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2cp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2cP.svg">R2-2cP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) shall be displayed below the Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign, except as provided in Paragraph 2 of this Section.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01a</span> <span class="ca">Where a special speed limit applies to certain classes of vehicles, the TRUCKS, 3 AXLES OR MORE 55 (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-3bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r6-3bca.webp">R6-3B(CA)</a>) sign  followed by the ALL VEHICLES WHEN TOWING 55 (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r6-4bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r6-4bca.webp">R6-4B(CA)</a>) sign may be used. Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b21">Section 2B.21</a> and CVC § 22406.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The legend of a Vehicle Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2P.svg">R2-2P</a> series) plaque may be combined in a single sign and displayed below the SPEED LIMIT XX legend, similar to the Combined Maximum and Minimum Speed Limits (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4a.svg">R2-4a</a>) sign (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b24">Section 2B.24</a>). A different vehicle class legend may be substituted on the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-2p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-2P.svg">R2-2P</a> series plaque for other classes of vehicles not included in Paragraph 1 of this Section.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b23" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.23 Night Speed Limit Plaque (R2-3P)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.23 &middot; manual page 179 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.23/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Where different speed limits are prescribed for day and night, both limits shall be posted.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> A Night Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-3p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-3P.svg">R2-3P</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) should be reversed using a white retroreflective legend and border on a black background.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> A Night Speed Limit plaque may be combined with or installed below the standard Speed Limit (R2- 1) sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Refer to CVC § 22355.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b24" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.24 Minimum Speed Limit Plaque (R2-4P) and Combined Maximum and Minimum Speed Limits Sign (R2-4a)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.24 &middot; manual page 180 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.24/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> A Minimum Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4P.svg">R2-4P</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) shall be displayed only in combination with a Speed Limit sign. Where used, the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4P.svg">R2-4P</a> plaque shall be mounted below a Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Where engineering judgment determines that slow speeds on a highway might impede the normal and reasonable movement of traffic, the Minimum Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4P.svg">R2-4P</a>) plaque may be installed below a Speed Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a>) sign to indicate the minimum legal speed. In lieu of a sign assembly with the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-1.svg">R2-1</a> sign and R2- 4P plaque, the Combined Maximum and Minimum Speed Limits (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-4a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-4a.svg">R2-4a</a>) sign may be used.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Refer to CVC § 22400.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b25" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.25 Higher Fines Signs and Plaque (R2-6P, R2-10, and R2-11)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.25 &middot; manual page 180 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.25/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Except as provided in Paragraph 3 of this Section, if increased fines are imposed for traffic violations within a designated zone of a roadway, a BEGIN HIGHER <span class="ca">DOUBLE</span> FINES ZONE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-10/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-10.svg">R2-10</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) or a FINES HIGHER<span class="ca">DOUBLE</span> (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-6ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-6aP.svg">R2-6aP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) shall be used to provide notice to road users.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If an <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-10/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-10.svg">R2-10</a> sign or an <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-6p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-6P.svg">R2-6P</a> plaque is posted to provide notice of increased fines for traffic violations, an END HIGHER <span class="ca">DOUBLE</span> FINES ZONE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-11/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-11.svg">R2-11</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>) shall be installed at the downstream end of the zone to provide notice to road users of the termination of the increased fines zone.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The BEGIN HIGHER <span class="ca">DOUBLE</span> FINES ZONE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-10/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-10.svg">R2-10</a>) sign or FINES HIGHER <span class="ca">DOUBLE</span> (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-6ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-6aP.svg">R2-6aP</a>) plaque may be omitted where the higher <span class="ca">double</span> fines zone is established by statute.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The BEGIN HIGHER <span class="ca">DOUBLE</span> FINES ZONE sign or FINES HIGHER<span class="ca">DOUBLE</span> plaque should be located at the beginning of the temporary traffic control zone, school zone, or other applicable designated zone and just beyond any interchanges, major intersections, or other major traffic generators.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Agencies should limit the use of the Higher <span class="ca">Double</span> Fines signs and plaque to locations where work is actually underway, or to locations where the roadway, shoulder, or other conditions, including the presence of a school zone and/or a reduced school speed limit zone, require a speed reduction or extra caution on the part of the road user.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> The Higher <span class="ca">Double</span> Fines signs and plaque shall have a black legend and border on a white rectangular background. All supplemental plaques mounted below the Higher <span class="ca">Double</span> Fines signs and plaque shall have a black legend and border on a white rectangular background.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The FINES HIGHER <span class="ca">DOUBLE</span> plaque shall be mounted below an applicable regulatory or warning sign in a temporary traffic control zone (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/6g/#6g08">Section 6G.08</a>), a school zone (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/7b/#7b06">Section 7B.06</a>), or other applicable designated zone.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> Alternate legends such as BEGIN (or END) DOUBLE FINES ZONE may also be used for the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-10/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-10.svg">R2-10</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-11/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-11.svg">R2-11</a> signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> The legend FINES HIGHER on the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-6p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-6P.svg">R2-6P</a> plaque may be replaced by FINES DOUBLE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-6ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-6aP.svg">R2-6aP</a>), $XX FINE (R2- 6bP), or another legend appropriate to the specific regulation (see <a href="#fig-2b-3">Figure 2B-3</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> The following may be mounted below an <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-10/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-10.svg">R2-10</a> sign or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-6p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-6P.svg">R2-6P</a> plaque: A.A supplemental plaque specifying the times that the higher fines are in effect (similar to the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/s4-1p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/S4-1P.svg">S4-1P</a> plaque shown in <a href="#fig-7b-1">Figure 7B-1</a>), B.A supplemental plaque WHEN CHILDREN (WORKERS) ARE PRESENT, or C.A supplemental plaque WHEN FLASHING (similar to the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/s4-4p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/S4-4P.svg">S4-4P</a> plaque shown in <a href="#fig-7b-1">Figure 7B-1</a>) if used in conjunction with a Speed Limit Sign Beacon (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/4s/#4s04">Section 4S.04</a>).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> In California, as per CVC only doubling of the fines is allowed, not higher fines of other denominations. Refer to CVC § 42010 for  Safety Enhancement-Double Fine Zone and <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/6g/#6g08">Section 6G.08</a> and CVC § 42009 for fines for offenses committed in highway  construction or maintenance area.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> The SPECIAL DRIVING ZONE BEGINS HERE – DOUBLE FINE ZONE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr53ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr53ca.webp">SR53(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-3ca">Figure 2B-3(CA)</a>) shall be  placed at the beginning of those portions of highways designated and identified as Safety Enhancement – Double Fine  Zones per CVC § 42010.</p>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> The SPECIAL DRIVING ZONE ENDS HERE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr55ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr55ca.webp">SR55(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-3ca">Figure 2B-3(CA)</a>) shall be placed at the end of those  portions of highways designated and identified as Safety Enhancement – Double Fine Zones per CVC § 42010.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> The DOUBLE FINE ZONE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr54ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr54ca.webp">SR54(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-3ca">Figure 2B-3(CA)</a>) should be placed at major intersections to those portions  of highways designated and identified as Safety Enhancement – Double Fine Zone, per CVC § 42010, to advise motorists upon  entering the highway that they are in a double fine zone.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Movement And Lane Control Signs And Plaques</div>


<span id="2b26" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.26 Movement Prohibition Signs (R3-1 through R3-4, R3-18, and R3-27)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.26 &middot; manual page 182 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.26/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">00</span> Motorists can make a semicircular or U-turn on a green signal or green arrow except where such turn is prohibited by   signs. Refer to CVC 21451 and 21454.</p>
<p><span class="pn">00a</span> <span class="ca">Local authorities, by ordinance, may prohibit the making of any turning movement by any vehicle at any intersection or   between any designated intersections. Refer to CVC 22113.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">00b</span> <span class="ca">Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b01">Section 2B.01</a> for information on ordinances and resolutions by local authorities that affect State highways.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">00c</span> <span class="ca">Caltrans may restrict turning movements on State highways. Refer to CVC 21352.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">00d</span> <span class="ca">A thorough investigation is necessary to determine whether or not the prohibited movements can be satisfactorily made at other  locations without undue circuitry of travel.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">00e</span> <span class="ca">Refer to CVC 22101 for Turn Prohibition signs.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Movement Prohibition signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) shall be installed where specific movements are prohibited at an intersection approach except as provided in Paragraphs 13 and 17 of this Section.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-inline single"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-4"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0233.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0233.webp" alt="Figure 2B-4. Movement Prohibition and Lane Control Signs and Plaques"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-4. Movement Prohibition and Lane Control Signs and Plaques <span class="src-page">p 233</span></figcaption></figure></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Movement Prohibition signs should only be used to prohibit a turn or through movement from an entire approach and should not be used to designate movements that are required or permitted from a specific lane or lanes on a multi- lane approach.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Movement Prohibition signs should be placed where they will be most easily seen by road users who might be intending to make the movement.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> If a No Right Turn (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-1.svg">R3-1</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) is used, at least one should be placed either over the roadway or at a right-hand corner of the intersection.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> If a No Left Turn (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-2.svg">R3-2</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) is used, at least one should <span class="ca">shall</span> be placed over the roadway, at the far left corner of the intersection, on a median, or in conjunction with the STOP sign or YIELD sign located on the near right corner.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Except as provided in Item C in Paragraph 11 of this Section for signalized locations, if a NO TURNS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-3.svg">R3-3</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) is used, two signs should be used, one at a location specified for a No Right Turn sign and one at a location specified for a No Left Turn sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">06a</span> <span class="ca">The NO TURNS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-3.svg">R3-3</a>) sign shall be used in advance of an intersection to indicate that turns are prohibited.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> If a No U-Turn (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-4.svg">R3-4</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) or a combination No U or Left Turn (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-18/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-18.svg">R3-18</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) is used, at least one should be used at a location specified for a No Left Turn sign.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07a</span> <span class="ca">The appropriate <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-4.svg">R3-4</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-18/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-18.svg">R3-18</a> sign should be placed as follows:  A. On undivided roads without traffic signals, place on the near right and far left corners of the intersection.  B. On undivided roads with traffic signals, place on the far right and far left corners of the intersection, or on the signal mast  arm.  C. On divided roads at both signalized and unsignalized intersections, place in the median on the near and far side of the  intersection, and on the signal mast arm at signalized intersections.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> If both left turns and U-turns are prohibited, the combination No U or Left Turn (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-18/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-18.svg">R3-18</a>) sign should <span class="ca">shall</span> be used instead of separate <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-2.svg">R3-2</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-4.svg">R3-4</a> signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">08a</span> <span class="ca">The No U-Turn (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-4.svg">R3-4</a>) sign shall be used where U turns are prohibited except when Intersection Lane Control signs  (R73(CA) Series) signs are used at signalized intersections with separate left turn phases.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">08b</span> <span class="ca">The R73(CA) series signs shall be installed adjacent to a signal face viewed by road users of the projection of the lane  to which it applies.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> Sections 2B.27 through 2B.30 contain information regarding lane control signs that indicate the required or permitted movements from individual lanes.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> If a No Straight Through (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-27/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-27.svg">R3-27</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) is used, at least one should be placed either over the roadway or at a location where it can be seen by road users who might be intending to travel straight through the intersection.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> If turn prohibition signs are installed in conjunction with traffic control signals: A.The No Right Turn sign should be installed adjacent to a signal face viewed by road users in the right-hand lane. B.The No Left Turn (or No U-Turn or combination No U or Left Turn) sign should be installed adjacent to a signal face viewed by road users in the left-hand lane. C.A NO TURNS sign should be placed adjacent to a signal face viewed by all road users on that approach, or two signs should be used.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> If turn prohibition signs are installed in conjunction with traffic control signals, an additional turn prohibition sign may be post-mounted to supplement the sign mounted overhead.</p>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> Where ONE WAY signs are used (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2b/#2b49">Section 2B.49</a>), No Left Turn and No Right Turn signs may be omitted.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">13a</span> <span class="ca">On one-way roads, the No Right/Left Turn (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-1.svg">R3-1</a>/<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-2.svg">R3-2</a>) sign shall be placed at the near right/left corner facing traffic  approaching the intersection.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> Where the movement restriction applies to certain vehicle classes, signs incorporating a supplementary legend, modified as appropriate, may be used to indicate the specific vehicle class restriction (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-1b/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-1b.svg">R3-1b</a> through <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-1d/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-1d.svg">R3-1d</a>) or exception (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-1e/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-1e.svg">R3-1e</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-1f/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-1f.svg">R3-1f</a>) (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>). When the movement restriction applies during certain time periods only, the following Movement Prohibition signing alternatives may be used and are listed in order of preference: A.A blank-out or changeable message sign (see Chapter 2L) that displays the prohibited movement only during the time that the movement prohibition is applicable, especially at signalized intersections. B.Permanently-mounted signs incorporating a supplementary legend showing the hours and days during which the prohibition is applicable (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-1g/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-1g.svg">R3-1g</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-1h/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-1h__Sheet_1_of_2.svg">R3-1h</a>) (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>). C.Portable signs, installed by proper authority, located off the roadway at each corner of the intersection. The portable signs are only to be used during the time that the movement prohibition is applicable.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> The blank-out part-time electronic-display Movement Prohibition sign shall consist of a red circle and diagonal with a white prohibited movement on an opaque black background.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> Movement Prohibition signs may be omitted at a ramp entrance to an expressway or a channelized intersection where the design is such as to indicate clearly the one-way traffic movement on the ramp or turning lane.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
