<p><span class="pn">05a</span> <span class="ca">“Bridge Out” refers to conditions where the bridge is destroyed, washed out, or submerged.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">05b</span> <span class="ca">“Bridge Closed” refers to situations where the bridge is closed temporarily for maintenance, construction, or other activities but  remains structurally intact.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> Where conditions allow for bicycle travel on the road beyond the point of closure to motor vehicles, an EXCEPT BICYCLES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r3-7bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R3-7bP.svg">R3-7bP</a>) plaque (see <a href="#fig-2b-4">Figure 2B-4</a>) may be used with the ROAD CLOSED sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> RAMP CLOSED (C2(CA)) sign should be installed where ramps or bridges have been closed to all traffic (except authorized  vehicles).</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The word BRIDGE may be substituted for RAMP where applicable.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b64" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.64 Weight Limit Signs (R12-1 through R12-7)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.64 &middot; manual page 219 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.64/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Weight limit signs (see <a href="#fig-2b-30">Figure 2B-30</a>) shall be used to indicate a section of highway or structure that has a vehicle weight restriction.</p>
<p><span class="pn">01a</span> <span class="ca">The weight limit signs shall be placed at each end of the affected portion of a highway section.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01b</span> <span class="ca">They should be placed at a distance of not more than 500 feet from the ends of an affected bridge or structure.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The units shown on any weight limit sign should be consistent within a State or region with respect to pounds or tons.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Where the restriction applies to axle weight rather than gross load, the legend AXLE WEIGHT LIMIT XX TONS or AXLE WEIGHT LIMIT XX LBS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-2.svg">R12-2</a>) may be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> In areas where multiple regulations are applicable, such as limiting both axle weight and gross vehicle weight, a WEIGHT LIMIT XX TONS PER AXLE, XX TONS GROSS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-4.svg">R12-4</a>) sign combining the necessary messages on a single sign may be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Posting of specific load limits may be accomplished by use of the Weight Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-5.svg">R12-5</a>) symbol sign. A sign containing the legend WEIGHT LIMIT on the top two lines, and showing up to three different truck symbols and their respective weight limits for which restrictions apply may be used, with the weight limits displayed to the right of each symbol as XX T. A bottom line of legend stating GROSS WT may be included if needed for enforcement purposes.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> A specialized hauling vehicle is a single unit truck with multiple closely-spaced axles. Examples include dump trucks, construction vehicles, solid waste trucks and other hauling trucks. Specialized hauling vehicles typically have 4 to 7 axles.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The Weight Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-6.svg">R12-6</a>) sign may be used to indicate vehicle weight restrictions for specialized hauling vehicles.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07a</span> <span class="ca">The Commercial Vehicle Weight Exclusion (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r36ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r36ca.webp">R36(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-30ca">Figure 2B-30(CA)</a>) may be used to indicate vehicles over  __tons are prohibited from certain streets and highways.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The symbols shown on the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-5.svg">R12-5</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-6.svg">R12-6</a> Weight Limit sign shall apply to all trucks of that configuration (single-unit, single-trailer or multi-trailer) regardless of the shape of the vehicle. Symbolic representations of other vehicle shapes or modifications of standard symbols shall not be used.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> The facility type (such as “BRIDGE”) may be added to the <span class="ca">beginning of the </span>legend of the sign to clarify the specific applicability of the weight limit.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> If the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-5.svg">R12-5</a> sign depicts only one single-unit vehicle symbol, the weight limit associated with that single-unit vehicle symbol shall apply to all single-unit vehicles, regardless of number of axles.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> The weight limit associated with the single-trailer vehicle symbol shall apply to all single-trailer vehicles, regardless of number of axles or vehicle shape.</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> The weight limit associated with the multi-trailer vehicle symbol shall apply to all multi-trailer vehicles with two or more trailers, regardless of number of axles or vehicle shape.</p>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> A weight limit sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-30">Figure 2B-30</a>) shall be located at the applicable section of highway or structure.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> An additional weight limit sign, with an advisory distance or directional legend, shall be located in advance of the applicable section of highway or structure so that prohibited vehicles can detour or turn around prior to the limit zone.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">14a</span> <span class="ca">Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c102ca">Section 2C.102(CA)</a> for warning Weight Limit signs used to select an alternate route.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> An emergency vehicle is designed to be used under emergency conditions to transport personnel and equipment to support the suppression of fires and mitigation of other hazardous situations. Emergency vehicles are typically operated by fire departments and are primarily equipped for firefighting, but are also used to respond to and mitigate other hazardous situations in an emergency. They can create higher load effects compared to non-emergency vehicles of similar weight.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> The Emergency Vehicle Weight Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-7.svg">R12-7</a>) sign carrying the legend EMERGENCY VEHICLE WEIGHT LIMIT SINGLE AXLE XX TONS, TANDEM XX TONS, and GROSS XX TONS may be used to indicate vehicle weight restrictions for emergency vehicles.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> When the emergency-vehicle weight limit is displayed in the same assembly as the primary weight limit sign, the Emergency Vehicle Weight Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r12-7ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R12-7aP.svg">R12-7aP</a>) plaque shall be mounted below.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">18</span> Refer to CVC 21101 through 21104 and 35650 through 35755 for Weight Limit signs.</p>
<p><span class="pn">19</span> The No Trucks (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r5-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R5-2.svg">R5-2</a>) sign is used together with a Truck Exclusion plaque (R20D(CA) Series) (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-12">Figure 2B-12</a> and Figure  2B-12(CA)) to specify the maximum weight limit in effect.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b65" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.65 Weigh Station Sign (R13-1)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.65 &middot; manual page 221 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.65/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> An <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r13-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R13-1.svg">R13-1</a> sign with the legend TRUCKS OVER XX TONS MUST ENTER WEIGH STATION NEXT RIGHT (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) should be used to direct appropriate traffic into an inspection station.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r13-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R13-1.svg">R13-1</a> sign should be supplemented by the D8 series of guide signs (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2d/#2d51">Section 2D.51</a>).</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> An <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr57-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr57-1ca.webp">SR57-1(CA)</a><span class="ca">sign with the legend</span><span class="ca">ALL TRUCKS STOP AT SCALES with NO PICKUPS <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr57-1pca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr57-1pca.webp">SR57-1P(CA)</a> mounted below (refer  to <a href="#fig-2b-31ca">Figure 2B-31(CA)</a>) should be used to direct appropriate traffic into a weigh station. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr57-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr57-1ca.webp">SR57-1(CA)</a> and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr57-1pca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr57-1pca.webp">SR57-1P(CA)</a> sign combination should be supplemented by the D8 series of guide signs (refer to Section  2D.51).</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-inline single"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-31"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0278.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0278.webp" alt="Figure 2B-31. Truck Signs R13-1 R14-1 R14-2 R14-3 R14-4 R14-5"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-31. Truck Signs R13-1 R14-1 R14-2 R14-3 R14-4 R14-5 <span class="src-page">p 278</span></figcaption></figure></div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Refer to <a href="#fig-2b-31ca">Figure 2B-31(CA)</a> and <a href="#fig-2e-59">Figure 2E-59</a> for Weigh Station Signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> The TRUCKS NOT GIVEN BYPASS SIGNAL MUST ENTER OPEN SCALES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr17ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr17ca.webp">SR17(CA)</a>) sign should be used in advance of a  truck weigh station that is equipped with a mainline bypass system and weigh-in-motion scales to electronically weigh and verify  compliance of commercial trucks as they approach the weigh station.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The Width Limit (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr40ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr40ca.webp">SR40(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-30ca">Figure 2B-30(CA)</a>) should be placed at truck weigh stations to direct over width  vehicles around the station, if the weigh station lacks adequate width. The California Highway Patrol should be contacted to  determine where these signs are needed. Refer to CVC 35790.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The ALL BUSES STOP AT SCALES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr41ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr41ca.webp">SR41(CA)</a>) and ALL BUSES with Arrow (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr42ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr42ca.webp">SR42(CA)</a>) signs shall be used as a  temporary sign for Critical Item Bus Inspections on state highways.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> The Weigh Station Repair Service (S21P(CA)) plaque may be installed at commercial vehicle inspection facilities on State  highways where needed at the request of the California Highway Patrol.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> The WAIT HERE UNTIL SCALE CLEAR (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr6-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr6-1ca.webp">SR6-1(CA)</a>) sign may be used at Weigh Stations to provide guidance to trucks  entering the scales.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> The RELEASE BRAKES WHILE ON SCALE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr7-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr7-1ca.webp">SR7-1(CA)</a>) sign may be used at Weigh Stations to provide guidance to trucks  when they are on the scales.</p>
<p><span class="pn">12</span> The SET PARKING BRAKES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr8-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr8-1ca.webp">SR8-1(CA)</a>) sign may be used at Weigh Stations to provide guidance to trucks when they are on  the scales.</p>
<p><span class="pn">13</span> The LOADED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr9-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr9-1ca.webp">SR9-1(CA)</a>) sign may be used at Weigh Stations to designate the lane loaded trucks are to use when passing  through the scales.</p>
<p><span class="pn">14</span> The EMPTY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr10-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr10-1ca.webp">SR10-1(CA)</a>) sign may be used at Weigh Stations to designate the lane empty trucks are to use when passing  through the scales.</p>
<p><span class="pn">15</span> The EMPTY 5 MPH (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr11-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr11-1ca.webp">SR11-1(CA)</a>) sign may be used at Weigh Stations to control the speed of empty trucks when passing  through scales.</p>
<p><span class="pn">16</span> The LOADED 3 MPH (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr12-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr12-1ca.webp">SR12-1(CA)</a>) sign may be used at Weigh Stations to control the speed of loaded trucks when passing  through scales.</p>
<p><span class="pn">17</span> The Theft CHP Plaque (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr13-1pca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr13-1pca.webp">SR13-1P(CA)</a>) may be used at Weigh Stations to advise scale users that removing any property from  the Weigh Station without authorization from the California Highway Patrol is a violation of the Penal Code.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b66" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.66 TRUCK ROUTE Sign (R14-1)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.66 &middot; manual page 221 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.66/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p>01 The TRUCK ROUTE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-1/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-1.svg">R14-1</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) should be used to mark a route that has been designated to allow truck traffic.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p>02 <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2d/#2d20">Section 2D.20</a> contains information regarding the use of the TRUCK (M4-4P) auxiliary plaque (see <a href="#fig-2d-5">Figure 2D-5</a>) on a designated numbered alternative route.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Refer to CVC §§ 21101 through 21104 and 35701 through 35715.</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Generally, Caltrans is not unilaterally authorized to prohibit truck travel on State highways. Various sections in the CVC allow  cities and counties to restrict, by ordinance, commercial vehicles subject to the specific conditions in those sections.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Generally, no such local ordinance is be effective with respect to any State highway until the ordinance has been approved by  Caltrans. This approval shall be made by the Caltrans Director.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> The proposed local ordinance shall designate an unrestricted alternate route, or routes, for use by the prohibited  vehicles. Such proposed local ordinances shall not be approved unless the alternate route, or routes, are considered  suitable by Caltrans.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> An investigation of designated alternate routes shall be made with special attention being given to the following  features:  1.<span class="ca"> Geometrics.  2.</span><span class="ca"> Increase in distance of travel and comparisons in time of travel.  3.</span><span class="ca"> Railroad grade crossings.  4.</span><span class="ca"> Present traffic and practical capacity of proposed alternates.  5.</span><span class="ca"> Structural adequacy of pavement for heavy truck traffic.  6.</span><span class="ca"> Heavy grades.  7.</span><span class="ca"> Proximity to schools or school routes.  8.</span><span class="ca"> Developed residential areas. </span></p>
</div>

<span id="2b67" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.67 Hazardous Material Signs (R14-2 and R14-3)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.67 &middot; manual page 222 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.67/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The Hazardous Material Route (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-2.svg">R14-2</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) may <span class="ca">shall</span>be used to identify routes that have been designated by proper authority for vehicles transporting hazardous material <span class="ca">and/or waste</span>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01a</span> <span class="ca">The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-2.svg">R14-2</a> sign may be used to guide road users around routes where the transportation of Hazardous Material and/or waste is  permitted.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01b</span> <span class="ca">The HAZARDOUS WASTE PERMITTED (R103aP(CA)) or the HAZARDOUS MATERIAL PERMITTED (R105aP(CA)) sign  shall be positioned below the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-3.svg">R14-3</a> symbol sign.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01d</span> <span class="ca">Refer to CVC §§ 31303 and 31304.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> On routes where the transporting of hazardous material <span class="ca">and/or waste</span> is prohibited, the Hazardous Material Prohibition (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-3.svg">R14-3</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) may <span class="ca">shall</span>be used.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02a</span> <span class="ca">The HAZARDOUS WASTE PROHIBITED (R102aP(CA)) or the HAZARDOUS MATERIAL PROHIBITED (R104aP(CA)) sign  shall be positioned below the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-2/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-2.svg">R14-2</a> sign.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02b</span> <span class="ca">Refer to CVC §§ 31303 and 31304.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> If used, the Hazardous Material Prohibition sign should be installed on a street or roadway at a point where vehicles transporting hazardous material have the opportunity to take an alternate route.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Refer to <a href="#fig-2b-31ca">Figure 2B-31(CA)</a> for Hazardous Waste/Material signs.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> The NO EXPLOSIVES OR FLAMMABLES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr18ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr18ca.webp">SR18(CA)</a>) sign should be placed on highways, structures, tunnels, etc. where  vehicles transporting explosives or flammable materials are prohibited. The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr18ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr18ca.webp">SR18(CA)</a> sign should be placed at a location that will  provide a motorist the opportunity to turn around.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> The EXPLOSIVES AND CORROSIVES PROHIBITED WITHOUT PERMIT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr19-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr19-1ca.webp">SR19-1(CA)</a>) sign should be placed on highways,  structures, tunnels, etc. where vehicles transporting explosives or corrosive materials are prohibited without a permit. The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr18ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr18ca.webp">SR18(CA)</a>  sign should be placed at a location that will provide a motorist the opportunity to turn around.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> The TRANSPORTING ILLEGAL FIREWORKS PROHIBITED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr25ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr25ca.webp">SR25(CA)</a>) sign may be used on any roadway upon which the  transportation of illegal fireworks have been prohibited by a local ordinance or resolution per California Health &amp; Safety Code  Division 11, Part 2, Sections 12500 through 12726.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> If used, the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr25ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr25ca.webp">SR25(CA)</a> sign shall be used to identify only those street or highway locations, either State or local, upon  which the transportation of illegal fireworks is prohibited, as established by the local ordinance or resolution.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b68" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.68 National Network Signs (R14-4 and R14-5)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.68 &middot; manual page 223 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.68/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The signing of the National Network routes for trucking is optional.<span class="ca"> Refer to Chapter 2I.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> When a National Network route is signed, the National Network (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-4.svg">R14-4</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) shall be used.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The National Network Prohibition (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-5.svg">R14-5</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-31">Figure 2B-31</a>) may be used to identify routes, portions of routes, and ramps where trucks are prohibited. The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r14-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R14-5.svg">R14-5</a> sign may also be used to mark the ends of designated routes.</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Other Regulatory Signs And Plaques</div>


<span id="2b69" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.69 Photo Enforced Signs and Plaques (R10-18, R10-18a, R10-19P, R10-19aP)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.69 &middot; manual page 224 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.69/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> A Traffic Laws Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18.svg">R10-18</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-32">Figure 2B-32</a>) may be installed at a jurisdictional boundary to advise road users that some of the traffic regulations within that jurisdiction are being enforced by photographic equipment.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-inline single"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-32"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0279.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0279.webp" alt="Figure 2B-32. Photo Enforcement Signs and Plaques R10-18 R10-18a R10-19P R10-19aP"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-32. Photo Enforcement Signs and Plaques R10-18 R10-18a R10-19P R10-19aP <span class="src-page">p 279</span></figcaption></figure></div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> A Traffic Signal Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18a.svg">R10-18a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-32">Figure 2B-32</a>) may <span class="ca">shall</span> be installed <span class="ca">within 200 feet</span> in advance of or at a traffic signal to advise road users that compliance with the signal is enforced by photographic equipment. <span class="ca">Refer to CVC §</span><span class="ca">21455.5. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02a</span> A Signal Ahead (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w3-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W3-3.svg">W3-3</a>) sign and a Traffic Signal Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18a.svg">R10-18a</a>) sign may be used on the same approach provided that they are on separate supports.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02b</span> <span class="ca">The RED LIGHT VIOLATION $ ___ FINE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr58ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr58ca.webp">SR58(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-3ca">Figure 2B-3(CA)</a>) may be used in advance of signalized  intersections where a local agency has adopted an ordinance setting a specific fine amount for red light violations within its  jurisdiction. The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr58ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr58ca.webp">SR58(CA)</a> sign may be placed on State highways when requested by the local agency.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> A Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-19p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-19P.svg">R10-19P</a>) plaque or a PHOTO ENFORCED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-19ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-19aP.svg">R10-19aP</a>) word message plaque (see Figure 2B- 32) may be mounted below a regulatory sign to advise road users that the regulation is being enforced by photographic equipment.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The Traffic Signal Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18a.svg">R10-18a</a>) sign shall not be installed on approaches to signalized locations where red-light cameras are not present on any of the approaches to the signalized location.</p>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> A Traffic Signal Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-18a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-18a.svg">R10-18a</a>) sign shall not be installed on the same support in combination with a Signal Ahead (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/w3-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/W3-3.svg">W3-3</a>) sign.</p>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> If used below a regulatory sign, the Photo Enforced (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-19p/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-19P.svg">R10-19P</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r10-19ap/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R10-19aP.svg">R10-19aP</a>) plaque shall be a rectangle with a black legend and border on a white background.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Refer to CVC § 21455.5 for Traffic Signal Automated Enforcement: Photographic Records.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b70" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.70 Move Vehicles from Travel Lanes Sign (R16-4)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.70 &middot; manual page 224 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.70/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> A STATE LAW MINOR CRASHES MOVE VEHICLES FROM TRAVEL LANES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-4.svg">R16-4</a>) <span class="ca">STATE LAW </span><span class="ca">MINOR  CRASH NO INJURIES – SAFELY MOVE VEHICLES FROM TRAVEL LANES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr61-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr61-1ca.webp">SR61-1(CA)</a> </span>sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-33">Figure 2B-33</a> <span class="ca">2B-33(CA)</span>) may be installed in accordance with the provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a01">Section 2A.01</a> to require motorists to move their vehicle out of the travel lanes if they have been involved in a <span class="ca">non-injury </span>crash.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If the specific requirements of a State law vary, the word legend of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-4.svg">R16-4</a> sign may be modified to reflect the appropriate law.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-inline single"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-33"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0280.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0280.webp" alt="Figure 2B-33. Other Regulatory Signs R16-15 R16-15a R16-3 R16-4 MINOR CRASH NO INJURIES SAFELY MOVE VEHICLES FROM TRAVEL LANES"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-33. Other Regulatory Signs R16-15 R16-15a R16-3 R16-4 MINOR CRASH NO INJURIES SAFELY MOVE VEHICLES FROM TRAVEL LANES <span class="src-page">p 280</span></figcaption></figure></div>

<span id="2b71" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.71 Move Over or Reduce Speed Sign (R16-3)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.71 &middot; manual page 224 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.71/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> A STATE LAW MOVE OVER OR REDUCE SPEED FOR VEHICLES STOPPED ON SHOULDER (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-3.svg">R16-3</a>) <span class="ca">STATE LAW MOVE OVER OR SLOW FOR STOPPED EMERGENCY AND MAINTENANCE VEHICLES  (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r110-1ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r110-1ca.webp">R110-1(CA)</a>)</span> sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-33">Figure 2B-33</a> <span class="ca">2B-33(CA)</span>) may be installed in accordance with the provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a01">Section 2A.01</a> to require motorists to change lanes and/or reduce speed when passing stopped emergency vehicles on the shoulder. <span class="ca">Refer  to CVC 21809.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If the specific requirements of a State law vary, the word legend of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-3/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-3.svg">R16-3</a> sign may be modified to reflect the appropriate law.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The R110(CA) sign shall only be used within freeway facilities.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b72" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.72 No Hand-Held Phone Use by Driver Signs (R16-15 and R16-15a)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.72 &middot; manual page 225 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.72/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> A STATE LAW NO HAND-HELD PHONE USE BY DRIVER (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-15/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-15.svg">R16-15</a> or <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-15a/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-15a.svg">R16-15a</a>) sign (see <a href="#fig-2b-33">Figure 2B-33</a>) may be installed in accordance with the provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a01">Section 2A.01</a> to notify drivers that they are prohibited from using hand-held telephones while driving.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> If the specific requirements of a State law vary, the word legend of the <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-15/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-15.svg">R16-15</a> series signs may be modified to reflect the appropriate law.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b73" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.73 Headlight Use Signs (R16-5 through R16-11)

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.73 &middot; manual page 225 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.73/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Some States require road users to turn on their vehicle headlights under certain weather conditions, as a safety improvement measure on roadways experiencing high crash rates, or in special situations such as when driving through a tunnel.</p>
<p><span class="pn">01a</span> <span class="ca">Refer to CVC § 24400.</span></p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> <a href="#fig-2b-34">Figure 2B-34</a> shows the various signs that can be used for informing motorists of these requirements.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> A LIGHTS ON WHEN USING WIPERS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-5.svg">R16-5</a>) sign or a LIGHTS ON WHEN RAINING (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-6/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-6.svg">R16-6</a>) sign may be installed in accordance with the provisions of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2a/#2a01">Section 2A.01</a> to inform road users of State laws regarding headlight use. Although these signs are typically installed facing traffic entering the State just inside the State border, they also may be installed at other locations within the State.</p>
</div>
<div class="mfig-inline single"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-2b-34"></span><span id="fig-2b-34ca"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0281.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0281.webp" alt="Figure 2B-34. Headlight Use Signs R16-5 R16-6 R16-7 R16-8 R16-9 R16-10 R16-11; Figure 2B-34(CA). California Headlight Use Signs S30-1(CA) DAYLIGHT HEADLIGHT SECTION"></a><figcaption>Figure 2B-34. Headlight Use Signs R16-5 R16-6 R16-7 R16-8 R16-9 R16-10 R16-11; Figure 2B-34(CA). California Headlight Use Signs S30-1(CA) DAYLIGHT HEADLIGHT SECTION <span class="src-page">p 281</span></figcaption></figure></div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> If a particular section of roadway has been designated as a safety improvement zone within which headlight use is required, a TURN ON HEADLIGHTS NEXT XX MILES (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-7/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-7.svg">R16-7</a>) sign or a BEGIN DAYTIME HEADLIGHT SECTION (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-10/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-10.svg">R16-10</a>) sign should be installed at the upstream end of the section, and an END DAYTIME HEADLIGHT SECTION (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-11/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-11.svg">R16-11</a>) sign should be installed at the downstream end of the section.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> A TURN ON HEADLIGHTS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-8/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-8.svg">R16-8</a>) sign may be installed to require road users to turn on their headlights in special situations such as when driving through a tunnel <span class="ca">or at the entrances from major side roads to a daytime headlight  section</span>. A CHECK HEADLIGHTS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-9/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-9.svg">R16-9</a>) sign may be installed downstream from the special situation to inform drivers that using their headlights is no longer required.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> A CHECK HEADLIGHTS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-9/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-9.svg">R16-9</a>) should be placed approximately 500 feet beyond the end of a daytime headlight section.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Daytime Headlight (S30(CA)) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-34ca">Figure 2B-34(CA)</a>) may be used after a traffic investigation and consultation with the  local CHP office and/or law enforcement as a traffic safety improvement measure in high accident locations on two lane highways  where there is a potential for head-on collisions.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> Refer to CVC § 21461 for enforcement of S30(CA) Series and <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-5/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-5.svg">R16-5</a> through <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r16-11/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R16-11.svg">R16-11</a> signs.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b74" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.74 Seat Belt Symbol

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.74 &middot; manual page 225 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.74/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The seat belt symbol should not be used alone. If used, the seat belt symbol should be incorporated into regulatory sign messages for mandatory seat belt use.</p>
<p><span class="pn">01a</span> <span class="ca">The Seat Belt (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr15ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr15ca.webp">SR15(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-33ca">Figure 2B-33(CA)</a>) should be placed in each direction on all freeways and other major  state routes at approximate 50 mile intervals.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01b</span> <span class="ca">The SAFETY BELT LAW ENFORCED (SR15aP(CA)) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-33ca">Figure 2B-33(CA)</a>) shall be placed below each  installation of the Seat Belt (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr15ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr15ca.webp">SR15(CA)</a>) sign.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01c</span> <span class="ca">The Seat Belt (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr15ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr15ca.webp">SR15(CA)</a>) and SAFETY BELT LAW ENFORCED (SR15A(CA)) sign combination may also be used on local  arterials.</span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The seat belt symbol is illustrated in the “Standard Highway Signs” publication (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/1a/#1a05">Section 1A.05</a>).</p>
</div>

<div class="subch">Barricades And Gates</div>


<span id="2b75" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.75 Barricades

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.75 &middot; manual page 226 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.75/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Barricades may be used to mark any of the following conditions: A.The end of a roadway, B.A ramp or lane that is closed for operational purposes, or C.The permanent or semi-permanent closure or termination of a roadway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> When used to warn and alert road users of the terminus of a roadway, other than in temporary traffic control zones, barricades shall meet the design criteria of <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/6k/#6k07">Section 6K.07</a> for a Type 3 Barricade, except that the colors of the stripes shall be retroreflective white and retroreflective red.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> An end-of-roadway marker or markers may be used as described in <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c73">Section 2C.73</a>.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Appropriate advance warning signs (see Chapter 2C) should be used.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b76" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.76 Gates

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.76 &middot; manual page 226 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.76/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> Gates described in this section used for weather or other emergency conditions are typically permanently installed to enable the gate to be immediately deployed as needed to prohibit the entry of traffic to the highway segment(s).</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> A gate typically features a gate arm that is moved from a vertical to a horizontal position or is rotated in a horizontal plane from parallel to traffic to perpendicular to traffic. Traffic is obstructed and required to stop when the gate arm is placed in a horizontal position perpendicular to traffic. Another type of gate consists of a segment of fence (usually on rollers) that swings open and closed, or that is retracted to open and then extended to close.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Gates are sometimes used to enforce a required stop. Some examples of such uses are the following: A.Parking facility entrances and exits, B.Private community entrances and exits, C.Military base entrances and exits, D.Toll plaza lanes, E.Movable bridges (see Chapter 4Q), F.Automated Flagger Assistance Devices (see Chapter 6L), and G.Grade crossings (see Part 8).</p>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> Gates are sometimes used to periodically close a roadway or a ramp. Some examples of such uses are the following: A.Closing ramps to implement counter-flow operations for evacuations, B.Closing ramps that lead to reversible lanes, and C.Closing roadways for weather events such as snow, ice, or flooding, or for other emergencies.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">05</span> Except as provided in Paragraph 6 of this Section, gate arms, if used, shall be fully retroreflective on both sides, have vertical stripes alternately red and white at 16-inch intervals measured horizontally as shown in <a href="#fig-8d-1">Figure 8D-1</a>. The width (which becomes the height of the retroreflective sheeting when the gate is in the down position) of the retroreflective sheeting on the front of the gate arm shall be at least 4 inches. <span class="ca">Refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/8d/#8d03">Section 8D.03</a>  Paragraph 5 if the Gate arm extends beyond 32 feet. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">06</span> If used on a one-way roadway or ramp, the retroreflective sheeting may be omitted on the side of the gate (or rolling fence) facing away from approaching traffic.</p>
<p><span class="pn">07</span> Where gate arms are used to block off ramps into reversible lanes or to redirect approaching traffic, the red and white striping may be angled such that the stripes slope downward at an angle of 45 degrees toward the side of the gate arm on which traffic is to pass.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">08</span> The gate arm shall extend across the approaching lane or lanes of traffic to effectively block motor vehicle, bicycle, and/or pedestrian travel as appropriate.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">09</span> When a gate that is rotated in a horizontal plane is in the position where it is parallel to traffic (indicating that the roadway is open), the outer end of the gate arm should be rotated to the downstream direction (from the perspective of traffic in the lane adjacent to the gate support) to prevent spearing if the gate is struck by an errant vehicle.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">10</span> If red lights are attached to a traffic gate, the red lights shall be steadily illuminated or flashed only during the period when the gate is in the horizontal or closed position and when the gate is in the process of being opened or closed.</p>
<p><span class="pn">11</span> Except as provided in Paragraph 6 of this Section, rolling sections of fence, if used, shall include either a horizontal strip of retroreflective sheeting on both sides of the fence with vertical stripes alternately red and white at 16-inch intervals measured horizontally to simulate the appearance of a gate arm in the horizontal position, or one or more Type 4 object markers (see <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/2c/#2c73">Section 2C.73</a>), or both. If a horizontal strip of retroreflective sheeting is used, the bottom of the sheeting shall be located 3.5 to 4.5 feet above the roadway surface.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b101ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.101(CA) NO FISHING (JUMPING) FROM BRIDGE Sign (R23(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.101(CA) &middot; manual page 227 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.101(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The NO FISHING (JUMPING) FROM BRIDGE sign (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r23ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r23ca.webp">R23(CA)</a>) (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-106ca">Figure 2B-106(CA)</a>) may be used when fishing or  jumping from a bridge is prevalent and where investigation has shown that fishing or jumping is unsafe or interferes with the orderly  movement of traffic.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b102ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.102(CA) $1000 Fine Signs (R47(CA) and R47A(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.102(CA) &middot; manual page 227 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.102(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The $1000 FINE FOR LITTERING (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r47ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r47ca.webp">R47(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-106ca">Figure 2B-106(CA)</a>) may be used to inform the public that it is  unlawful to dispose of litter on the highway.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> Refer to Streets and Highway Code Section 101.6 and CVC 23111 through 23113 and 42001.7.</p>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The $1000 FINE FOR ANIMAL ABANDONMENT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r47aca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r47aca.webp">R47A(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-106ca">Figure 2B-106(CA)</a>) is used to inform the public that  the abandonment or dumping of any animal is a criminal offense.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r47aca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r47aca.webp">R47A(CA)</a> sign should be placed on all major state highways, as close as practicable, following the Welcome to California  (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/g10bca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/g10bca.webp">G10B(CA)</a>) sign.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b103ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.103(CA) PRIVATE ROAD (PRIVATE PROPERTY) VEHICLE CODE ENFORCED Sign (R101(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.103(CA) &middot; manual page 227 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.103(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The PRIVATE ROAD VEHICLE CODE ENFORCED (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r101ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r101ca.webp">R101(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-106ca">Figure 2B-106(CA)</a>) shall be used at the  entrance to a privately owned and maintained road when enforcement of vehicle provisions apply, as provided in CVC<span class="ca">§  21107.7. </span></p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The alternate message PRIVATE PROPERTY shall be used at each entrance to a privately owned and maintained off- street parking facility when enforcement of vehicle code provisions apply, as provided in CVC<span class="ca">§</span><span class="ca"> 21107.8. </span></p>
</div>

<span id="2b104ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.104(CA) GOLF CARTS OK DAYLIGHT HOURS Sign (SR43(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.104(CA) &middot; manual page 227 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.104(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The GOLF CARTS OK DAYLIGHT HOURS (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr43ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr43ca.webp">SR43(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-106ca">Figure 2B-106(CA)</a>) shall be placed on roadways  which local authorities have designated for combined use in accordance with CVC<span class="ca">§ </span><span class="ca">21115. </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The ordinance number may be included on the sign.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b105ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.105(CA) Bus and Truck Registration Sign (SR44(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.105(CA) &middot; manual page 228 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.105(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The Bus and Truck Registration (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr44ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr44ca.webp">SR44(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-106ca">Figure 2B-106(CA)</a>) should be placed at all Border Inspections  Stations to relay this information to Interstate carriers.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b106ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.106(CA) EMERGENCY ACCESS KEEP CLEAR Sign (SR46(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.106(CA) &middot; manual page 228 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.106(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The EMERGENCY ACCESS KEEP CLEAR (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr46ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr46ca.webp">SR46(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-106ca">Figure 2B-106(CA)</a>) may be used where there is traffic  back up due to a controlled intersection or cross street that affects access to the driveway of any emergency service facility such as  fire, police or ambulance. Refer to CVC § 22500(d) and 22526.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr46ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr46ca.webp">SR46(CA)</a> sign shall be used in conjunction with KEEP CLEAR pavement markings (refer to <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/ca-mutcd/3b/#3b26">Section 3B.26</a>) that  delineate the limits of the keep clear area.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The <a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr46ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr46ca.webp">SR46(CA)</a> signs may be placed on both ends of the keep clear area.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">04</span> However, if only one sign is used, it should be placed on the upstream side.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b107ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.107(CA) Off Highway Vehicle Signs (SR47(CA) and SR48(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.107(CA) &middot; manual page 228 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.107(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The OFF HIGHWAY VEHICLE COMBINED USE NEXT (X MILES) (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr47ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr47ca.webp">SR47(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-106ca">Figure 2B-106(CA)</a>) should be  used to inform motorists of the length of an Off Highway Vehicle Combined Use segment of the highway.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The NO OFF HIGHWAY VEHICLES BEYOND THIS POINT (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/sr48ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/sr48ca.webp">SR48(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-106ca">Figure 2B-106(CA)</a>) should be placed at  the end of an Off Highway Vehicle Combined Use segment of the highway.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b108ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.108(CA) NO CAMPING OR SLEEPING IN VEHICLE Sign (R119(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.108(CA) &middot; manual page 228 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.108(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov gui"><div class="prov-label">Guidance</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The NO CAMPING OR SLEEPING IN VEHICLE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r119ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r119ca.webp">R119(CA)</a>) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-106">Figure 2B-106</a>) may be used to inform the public that  it is unlawful to camp or sleep in the vehicle in the right of way of a state highway or local roadway where there is an adopted  resolution or ordinance that prohibits camping or sleeping in vehicle and allows use of the sign. The resolution or ordinance number  and adopting agency shall be included on the bottom of the sign.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The<span class="ca">NO CAMPING OR SLEEPING IN VEHICLE</span><span class="ca"> (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r119ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r119ca.webp">R119(CA)</a>) sign may have a supplemental plaque (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r2-6bp/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R2-6bP.svg">R2-6bP</a>) installed below the  sign for the applicable fine amount per the adopted resolution or ordinance (e.g., $XXXX FINE). </span></p>
</div>
<div class="prov sup"><div class="prov-label">Support</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> Do not use the NO CAMPING OR SLEEPING IN VEHICLE (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r119ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/r119ca.webp">R119(CA)</a>) sign when there is no adopted resolution or ordinance to  support its use or enforcement of the sign.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b109ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.109(CA) BEGIN/END FREEWAY Sign (R57(CA) and R58(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.109(CA) &middot; manual page 228 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.109(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The BEGIN FREEWAY (R57(CA)) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-25ca">Figure 2B-25(CA)</a>) shall be used to mark the beginning of freeway.</p>
<p><span class="pn">02</span> The R57(CA) sign shall be placed above the EMERGENCY PARKING ONLY (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/r8-4/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/R8-4.svg">R8-4</a>) sign. Refer to CVC 21960.</p>
</div>
<div class="prov std"><div class="prov-label">Standard</div>
<p><span class="pn">03</span> The END FREEWAY (R58(CA)) sign (refer to <a href="#fig-2b-25ca">Figure 2B-25(CA)</a>) shall be used to mark the end of a freeway.</p>
</div>

<span id="2b110ca" class="anchor"></span>

## 2B.110(CA) No Loitering, Camping, Vending or Parking of Vehicles 30 Feet or Longer Sign (S22(CA))

<div class="secmeta">Section 2B.110(CA) &middot; manual page 228 &middot; <a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/s/ca/2B.110(CA)/">short link</a></div>

<div class="prov opt"><div class="prov-label">Option</div>
<p><span class="pn">01</span> The NO LOITERING, CAMPING, VENDING OR PARKING OF VEHICLES 30 FEET OR LONGER (<a class="sref" href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/s22ca/" data-svg="/SoCal-Super-Standards/signs/ca/s22ca.webp">S22(CA)</a>) sign (refer to  <a href="#fig-2b-106ca">Figure 2B-106(CA)</a>) may be placed at fringe and transportation corridor parking facilities constructed, maintained, or operated by  Caltrans for the purpose of ridesharing. Refer to CVC Section 22518.</p>
</div>

<h2 id="figures-tables">Figures and tables</h2>

<div class="mfig-grid"><figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-p157"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0157.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0157.webp" alt="Table page 157"></a><figcaption>Table page 157 <span class="src-page">p 157</span></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-p229"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0229.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0229.webp" alt="Figure page 229"></a><figcaption>Figure page 229 <span class="src-page">p 229</span></figcaption></figure>

<figure class="mfig"><span id="fig-p304"></span><a href="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0304.webp"><img loading="lazy" src="/SoCal-Super-Standards/figs/ca-mutcd/p0304.webp" alt="Table page 304"></a><figcaption>Table page 304 <span class="src-page">p 304</span></figcaption></figure><details class="mtab-wrap"><summary>Table as text (page 304, searchable; check against the image)</summary><div class="mtab-scroll"><table class="mtab"><thead><tr><th>Category</th><th>Factors</th><th></th><th></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Crash Weighting Factors to Develop One Serious/Fatal Injury Safety Corridor</td><td></td><td>Crash severity: Fatal Crashes, Serious Injury Crashes ▪</td><td></td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td>Mode: Pedestrian-bicycle related crashes, vehicle/other ▪</td><td></td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td>▪ Disadvantaged Community Status: MPO/RTPA or locally defined disadvantaged</td><td></td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td>community status based on most current version of CalEnviroScreen</td><td></td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td>Vulnerable Populations: Seniors (age 65 and older) and Youth (under age 15) based on ▪</td><td></td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td>the American Community Survey</td><td></td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td>▪ School proximity (within 0.25 miles) based on the California School Campus Database</td><td></td></tr><tr><td></td><td></td><td>Systemic or Predicted Crashes ▪</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Crash Density</td><td>Each roadway segment block may be converted into ~ 0.25-mile overlapping or contiguous “corridor” segments to create a consistent unit of measurement and assess the concentration of linear patterns of crashes within a defined distance. The highest scoring (i.e., most fatal and serious injury crashes per mile) “corridor” segments within a street need to be identified and an appropriate threshold set to determine safety corridor eligibility.</td><td></td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Maintenance</td><td>The jurisdiction may establish a review and re-evaluation frequency for safety corridors. However, such frequency need not exceed seven years.</td><td></td><td></td></tr></tbody></table></div></details>
